115年 9月全國賦稅收入初步統計 
一、全國賦稅收入 
（一）本月數 
9 月實徵淨額 4,965 億元，較上年同月增加 813 億元(+19.6%)。 
（二）累計數 
累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 3 兆 5,714 億元，較上年同期增加 7,004 億
元(+24.4%)。 
（三）達成率 
1.中央政府總預算業於 9 月 18 日公布，稅課收入較原預算案提高
6,000 億元，併計地方政府及特種基金後，全國稅課收入較預算案
提高 5,852 億元，本次發布均改依法定數計算預算達成率(實徵淨
額占全年預算數)。 
2.累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額占累計分配預算數 115.0%，占全年預算數
78.8%。 
圖 1 全國賦稅收入及年增率

說明：115 年 5-7 月年增率跳動較大，係因 114 年所得稅結算申報及繳納期限延長至 6 月底所致。

二、主要稅目別變動
（一）9 月實徵淨額與上年同月比較，以營業稅增加 297 億元、營利事業
所得稅增加 254 億元及證券交易稅增加 222 億元較多。
（二）累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額與上年同期比較，以證券交易稅增加 3,077
億元、營利事業所得稅增加 1,853 億元及營業稅增加 1,203 億元較多。
圖 2 主要稅目別變化
(115 年 1-9 月)
1.營利事業所得稅
(1)9 月實徵淨額 2,007 億元，較上年同月增加 254 億元(+14.5%)，主
因暫繳稅款成長。
(2)累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 1 兆 28 億元，較上年同期增加 1,853 億元
(+22.7%)，主因結算申報自繳 、暫繳稅款 俱增及延分期稅款陸續
入帳。
2.綜合所得稅
9 月實徵淨額 490 億元，較上年同月增加 34 億元(+7.4%)，主因扣繳
稅款增加所致；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 8,279 億元，較上年同期增加
790 億元(+10.6%)，主因結算申報自繳增加，以及盈餘分配、薪資所

得等扣繳稅款均呈成長。
3.營業稅
9月實徵淨額1,414億元，較上年同月增加297億元(+26.6%)，累計1至
9月實徵淨額6,230億元，較上年同期增加1,203億元(+23.9%)，均因內
需穩定，加以電子零組件 、資通與視聽產品 等進口代徵稅額增加 ，
惟出口暢旺，適用外銷零稅率營業人申請退稅亦增，抵銷部分增幅。

### Page 3

3
4.貨物稅
(1)9月實徵淨額100億元，較上年同月減少11億元(-10.0%)，主因汽柴
油減徵幅度擴大，惟電動車大量到港推升進口 車稅額，抵銷部分
減幅。
(2)累計1至9月實徵淨額874億元，較上年同期減少207億元(-19.2%)，
除汽柴油降稅因素外 ，亦與新購汽機車及電動車之減免徵退稅增
加有關。
5.證券交易稅
(1)9 月及累計 1 至 9 月上市上櫃股票平均每日成交值為 1 兆 317 億
元、1 兆 1,461 億元，分別較上年同期(6,006 億元、4,451 億元)增
71.8%、1.6 倍。
(2)9 月 證 交 稅 實 徵 淨 額534 億 元， 較 上 年 同 月 增 加 222 億 元
(+71.2%)；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 5,060 億元，較上年同期增加
3,077 億元(+1.6 倍)。
6.關稅
9 月實徵淨額 157 億元，較上年同月增加 3 億元(+1.9%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 1,224 億元，較上年同期增加 80 億元(+7.0%)，均因機械

及電機設備、基本金屬及其製品等進口稅額增加。
7.贈與稅
9 月實徵淨額 23 億元，較上年同月增加 6 億元(+35.7%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 211 億元，較上年同期增加 43 億元(+25.3%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加。
8.土地增值稅
9 月實徵淨額 56 億元，較上年同月增加 12 億元(+26.6%)，主因大額
稅款案件增加；累計 1 至 9 月實徵淨額 504 億元，較上年同期減少
14 億元(-2.8%)，主因房地交易量及大額稅款案件減少。
9.印花稅
9 月實徵淨額 31 億元，較上年同月增加 4 億元(+16.2%)，累計 1 至 9
月實徵淨額 175 億元，較上年同期增加 15 億元(+9.0%)，主因銀行放
款稅額成長。
（三）前 9月主要稅目實徵淨額占分配預算數之比率：
1.大於 100%者有證交稅(272%)、遺產稅(155%)、贈與稅(132%)、印花
稅(130%)、營業稅(116%)、關稅(114%)、契稅(111%)、房屋稅(106%)、

綜所稅(101%)、牌照稅(101%)及營所稅(101%)等。
2.低於 100%者有貨物稅(98%)、菸酒稅(97%)、土增稅(85%)、地價稅
(84%)及特銷稅(57%)等。

三、各級政府賦稅收入概況
(一)中央政府
累計 1 至 9 月中央政府賦稅收入 (不含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵淨 額
2 兆 3,603 億元(占分配預算數 119.3%)，較上年同期增加 2,553 億元
(+12.1%)，雖新版財政收支劃分法 開始施行，營業稅劃歸中央政府 比
率大幅調降，減少 2,748 億元(-90.7%)，惟證交稅、所得稅分別增加 3,077
億元、2,230 億元，互抵後呈現上升。
(二)地方政府
累計 1 至 9 月地方政府賦稅收
入(含中央統籌分配稅款 )實徵
淨額 1 兆 898 億元(占分配預
算數 109.4%)，較上年同期增
加 4,527 億元(+71.0%)，主因
納入中央統籌分配 之營業稅
增加 4,030 億元(+2.1 倍)所致。
(三)中央特種基金
累計 1 至 9 月撥入中央特種基金之健康福利捐實徵淨額 195 億元，較
上年同期增加 2 億元(+0.8%)；房地合一課徵所得稅撥入住宅基金 135
億元，較上年同期減少 7 億元(-4.7%)；撥入長照基金 883 億元(其中 541

億元來自房地合一課徵所得稅、195 億元來自菸稅，146 億元來自遺贈
稅)，較上年同期增加 8 億元(+0.9%)。

Update
E Ink Holdings Inc.  | Asia Pacific
September  Sales +5% MoM/
+14% YoY
In this report, we focus on E Ink's monthly sales, which we
believe could be a catalyst for its share price.
Details:
• September sales were NT$3,613mn (+5% MoM/+14% YoY).
• 3Q26 sales came in at NT$9,936mn (-3% QoQ/-5% YoY), in line with our
estimate at NT$9,985mn (-2% QoQ/-4% YoY) and the consensus at NT
$9,774mn (-4% QoQ/-6% YoY).
Our view:
• E Ink's monthly sales run rate will need to stay at the September level

through year end to achieve its full-year revenue guidance of up 10-15% YoY
for 2026. This implies its 4Q26 revenue will be up by 9% QoQ, which still
looks within reach, despite the softer seasonality
• While the worst seems to be behind us and valuation is at a trough level of
12x 2027e P/E, we see no strong catalysts in the near term, despite our long-
term positive view on  e-paper demand across various applications. Stay EW.
Exhibit 1: E Ink's monthly sales vs. its share price since 2015

Valuation Methodology and Risks
E Ink Holdings Inc. (8069.TWO)
Base case, 12x 2027e P/E. We believe this valuation methodology best captures E Ink's earn-
ings growth momentum. We view our target multiple of 12x as fair, considering our estimated
17% earnings CAGR for 2025-28. The implied PEG of 0.7x is largely in line with its typical level
in 2022-25, when the stock was trading at an average P/E of 22x one-year forward consensus
EPS with a 37% earnings CAGR.

Risks to Upside
n Firmer-than-expected ESL orders
n Stronger demand for e-readers and e-notes
n Better macro conditions
n Milder competition
n Brisker inventory digestion
Risks to Downside
n Softer-than-expected ESL orders
n Slower demand for e-readers and e-notes
n Macro headwinds
n Intensified competition
n More protracted inventory digestion

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

3-month preview: The company’s Sep revenues were up 28% YoY/10% MoM to 
NT$224m, though 27% below our previous estimates, we attribute this to the 
longer-than-expected time to see high-speed CPO FAU ramp up. Looking ahead, we 
expect to see sequential MoM increase at 15%/20% MoM in Oct/Nov 2026 to 
NT$258m/ NT$309m, supported by capacity expansion, efficiency improvement, 
and rack-level AI server ramp up.

### Page 2

Earnings revision: Factoring in FOCI’s 3Q26 revenues, we revise down 2026E earnings 
by 6% on lower-than-expected CPO FAU revenues in 3Q, and revise up 2027E-30E 
earnings by 1%/1%/1%/1% mainly on higher revenues of high-speed CPO FAU, 
supported by FOCI’s focus on CPO-scale up and rising CPO switch demand.

Valuation: We continue to derive our 12m target price via a discounted P/E and derive 
the target P/E multiple via peers’ trading P/E vs. forward year EPS YoY (methodology 
unchanged). With this, our target multiple declines slightly to 16.4x (vs. 15.4x previously) 
based on FOCI’s largely unchanged long-term earnings growth (2030E-31E EPS YoY at 
15% on avg.), reflecting improving sentiment on rack-level AI server ramp up and 
high-speed transition. We continue to apply the target P/E to 2030E EPS (unchanged), 
and discount it back to 2027E via a 12.6% COE (unchanged). Our new TP is NT$900 (vs. 
NT$833 previously). Our new TP implies 51x 2027E P/E. Our new TP implies 10x 2027E 
P/S, within its historical trading range of 5x to 30x. Maintain Buy.

Ratings, coverage universe and related definitions
Buy (B), Neutral (N), Sell (S) Analysts recommend stocks as Buys or Sells for inclusion on various regional Investment Lists. Being assigned a Buy or Sell
on an Investment List is determined by a stock’s total return potential relative to its coverage universe. Any stock not assigned as a Buy or a Sell on an
Investment List with an active rating (i.e., a  stock that is not Rating Suspended, Not Rated, Early-Stage Biotech, Coverage Suspended or Not Covered), is

deemed Neutral. Each region manages Regional Conviction Lists, which are selected from Buy rated stocks on the respective region’s Investment Lists
and represent investment recommendations focused on the size of the total return potential and/or the likelihood of the realization of the return across
their respective areas of coverage.  The addition or removal of stocks from such Conviction Lists are managed by the Investment Review Committee or
other designated committee in each respective region and do not represent a change in the analysts’ investment rating for such stocks.

Total return potential represents the upside or downside differential between the current share price and the price target, including all paid or
anticipated dividends, expected during the time horizon associated with the price target. Price targets are required for all covered stocks. The total
return potential, price target and associated time horizon are stated in each report adding or reiterating an Investment List membership.
Coverage Universe: A list of all stocks in each coverage universe is available by primary analyst, stock and coverage universe at

https://www.gs.com/research/hedge.html.
Not Rated (NR). The investment rating, target price and earnings estimates (where relevant) are removed pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when
Goldman Sachs is acting in an advisory capacity in a merger or in a strategic transaction involving this company, when there are legal, regulatory or
policy constraints due to Goldman Sachs’ involvement in a transaction, and in certain other circumstances. Early-Stage Biotech (ES). An investment

rating and a target price are not assigned pursuant to Goldman Sachs policy when this company has neither a drug, treatment or medical device that
has passed a Phase II clinical trial nor a license to distribute a post-Phase II drug, treatment or medical device.  Rating Suspended (RS). Goldman Sachs
Research has suspended the investment rating and price target for this stock, because there is not a sufficient fundamental basis for determining an
investment rating or target price. The previous investment rating and target price, if any, are no longer in effect for this stock and should not be relied

upon.  Coverage Suspended (CS). Goldman Sachs has suspended coverage of this company.  Not Covered (NC). Goldman Sachs does not cover this
company.

Chenbro (8210.TW): Chassis and Racks in expansion on AI infrastructure 
upcycle; Neutral
12 October 2026 | 2:24AM HKT
We are positive on Chenbro’s leading market position in AI server chassis, riding on AI 
capex across US and China cloud service providers (CSPs), and expanding to full rack 
business (e.g. noise-cancellation racks, liquid cooling CDU racks, etc.). Chenbro’s 
revenues in Sep increased by 1% MoM, and we expect the sequential increase to 
continue in Oct-Dec, bringing 4Q26E revenue growth to +58% YoY / +44% QoQ. 
Catalysts in 4Q26 include: (1) major ASIC AI servers customers’ new models ramp-up, 
(2) rack business expansion on noise-cancellation racks ramping up and expansion to 
liquid cooling CDU racks, (3) AI servers powered by NVIDIA (e.g. HGX, MGX) in 
expansion with new chipset platforms, and (4) ongoing capacity expansion to 
support the revenues growth. Maintain Neutral on fair valuation.

Earnings revision

We lower net income by 8%/3%/4% in 2026-28E mainly on lower revenues to reflect the 
less-than-expected shipment of AI servers chassis and the 3Q26 revenues miss, which 
were 22%/15% lower than our estimates / Bloomberg consensus. We lower revenues by 
7%/5%/6% in 2026-28E.

Compared to Bloomberg consensus, we are 1%/12%/27% ahead on 2026E/ 27E/28E 
net income, reflecting our positive view on Chenbro’s revenues expansion.

### Page 3

Valuation

We continue to use near-term P/E to derive our 12m TP . We derive our target P/E 
multiple from (1) peers’ avg. ratio of trading P/E to forward year fundamentals (NI YoY 
and OPM), which is at 0.3x, and (2) Chenbro’s forward year fundamentals: 2027-28E NI 
YoY now at 47% on avg. and OPM is at 26.8% on avg. Our new target P/E multiple is at 
21.2x (vs. 20.2x of our previous TP implied 2027E P/E). Our previous target P/E multiple 
to forward year fundamentals was also 0.3x. Our new target P/E multiple of 21.2x is 
between the company’s avg.+1stv. P/E of 17x and peak P/E of 26x, reflecting our 
positive view on its expansion toward AI servers. Accordingly, we raise our target price 
by 1.7% to NT$1,507 (21.2x 2027E P/E) from NT$1,482.

## Document 141: 261018_ms_OCP-takeaways

Source PDF: all report pdf/261018_ms_OCP-takeaways.pdf

### Page 1

Greater China Technology Hardware
Asia Pacific
Industry View In-Line
CPO/NPO and micro     VCSEL based   optical solutions are the
focus for networking, while HVDC takes center stage for power.
Higher bandwidth and better power efficiency: In the networking space, the
migration into higher bandwidth continues, as players like Accton and Celestica
showcased their 1.6T network switches. We think a bigger theme is the effort to
improve the power efficiency of current mainstream pluggable optical solutions.

First off, one well-discussed approach is the adoption CPO or NPO, and it seems the
latter is gaining more attention due to the technical challenges in the former. Spec
standardization, such as CPX connectors, could further accelerate the proliferation,
in our view. Secondly, copper could still help, as Credo has its 1.6T AEC cables for
inter-tray transmission while many other vendors (Amphenol, TE Connectivity,
Molex, etc.) have their 448G CPC solutions for in-tray transmission. Lastly, the micro

VCSEL based optical modules from Credo and ALAB represent another power
efficient solution (12W/end at 1,6T), which has reliability comparable to copper
(100M hours of MTBF/MTBI) for a transmission distance of 50-100m. We believe
this micro VCSEL based solution could potentially replace the AEC for shorter
distance transmission when copper hits the physical limit in the medium term and
be integrated with the CPO architecture in the longer term. In sum, we believe the

above-mentioned solutions are likely to co-exist for different use cases.
800VDC for power delivery taking the center stage: While the Vera Rubin NVL72
rack already has the in-rack power shelf reference design (110KW/shelf at 3+1
redundancy), Nvidia unveiled another reference design this time, using one 660KW
800VDC power rack to support two Vera Rubin NVL72 racks. Moreover, we also
saw a few ±400VDC reference designs as well. Our conversations with companies

on the exhibition floor indicate that the HVDC adoption should start from late
2026, but likely at a small scale extending into 2027. The next checkpoint will be
Vera Rubin Ultra racks, where increasing power consumption and more hardware
devices for inter-rack scale-up network might take up more pace on the rack, though
the liquid cooled power shelf  should ease the space budget a bit. We believe both
solutions are progressing and will closely monitor the dynamics.

Stock picks: We believe that Bizlink (3665.TW) should be a beneficiary of the
proliferation of the micro VCSEL based optical solution, as it is the assembly partner
for Credo and potentially others as well. Chroma (2360.TW) should see drivers
from power testing, CPU/GPU/TPU SL T/FT, and photonics testing (pluggable/
CPO)tools. Delta( (2308.TW) and Lite-On (2301.TW) should continue to grow with
the increasing power consumption of AI clusters, but more meaningful growth

communications with a subject company, public appearances

CPX and NPX all marching forward
Though CPO remains as the holy grail in the space, its yield issue and lower serviceability
still present challenges to the wider adoption in the near term. We believe that the efforts
will continue, but the industry is simultaneously pursuing other more practical solutions

to sort out the power consumption issue of the existing pluggable transceivers,  including
NPO (near-packaged optics), CPC (co-packaged copper) and NPC (near-packaged copper)
Compared to CPO, NPO compromises on the power consumption, but in exchange it eases
the stringent requirement on alignment and serviceability. Given its better technology
readiness and more mature supply chain, we think it might see good traction in the
coming years.

VCSEL based optical solutions gaining more attention
In addition to CPO/NPO/CPC/NPC designs, we see the micro VCSEL based optical

current generation can enable 32Gbps of bandwidth per lane and the 100-200Gbps of
bandwidth per lane is in the roadmap.

In sum, different solutions of data transmission are emerging, trying to improve the power

combination of cost and benefits.

Power becoming another bottleneck in the AI deployment
HVDC adoption emerging from Vera Rubin
In the power space, HVDC has been one of the key focuses in the AI cluster, as the rapid
increase in AI rack power density is creating growing challenges for conventional power-
distribution architectures. As rack-level power consumption rising from tens of kilowatts
to several hundreds of KWs, electrical losses, cable size, thermal management
requirements, and overall system complexity increase significantly. Under traditional low-

voltage power architectures, delivering larger amounts of power requires proportionally
higher current, which increases resistive losses and places greater stress on cables,
connectors, busbars, and power-conversion equipment.
At the same time, every power-conversion stage introduces efficiency losses and
generates additional heat. As AI deployments scale into multi-megawatt and eventually
gigawatt-level campuses, even small efficiency improvements can translate into

### Page 10

M
Idea
10
meaningful reductions in operating costs, cooling requirements, and infrastructure
investment. Consequently, the industry is increasingly focused on power architectures
that can reduce conversion stages, lower current levels, and improve overall power-
delivery efficiency.
Against this backdrop, high-voltage direct current (HVDC) is emerging as one of the most
important architectural upgrades for next-generation AI infrastructure. By increasing

distribution voltage, HVDC allows the same amount of power to be delivered at a lower
current, reducing conduction losses and improving system efficiency. Higher-voltage
architectures also improve scalability for ultra-high-density rack deployments by
alleviating some of the physical and thermal constraints associated with conventional
power-distribution systems.
Exhibit 18: Better power efficiency for 800VDC architecture
Source: Delta
Exhibit 19: Better efficiency leads to cost savings

Source: Delta
In a joint keynote speech by Delta and Nvidia, the roadmap illustrates the industry's
gradual migration toward higher-voltage power architectures. Earlier AI server generations
primarily utilized conventional power-delivery schemes centered around rack-level 48V
distribution. As rack power requirements increased with Blackwell-generation systems,
industry participants began evaluating higher-voltage architectures capable of supporting

substantially greater power density. Looking ahead, the industry is increasingly converging
around 800VDC as a potential next-generation standard for large-scale AI deployments,
particularly as rack-level power consumption approaches several hundred kilowatts. The
transition reflects the need to improve power-distribution efficiency while supporting the
continued growth of AI computing infrastructure.
Though we also saw the 800VDC power rack reference design for the Vera Rubin rack in a

1-to-2 configuration (one 660KW power rack supporting two Vera Rubin racks), we think
the adoption of this solution might be relatively low, since the Vera Rubin rack also has a
reference design for the in-rack power shelf design

### Page 11

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 11
Exhibit 20: 800VDC roadmap
Source: Delta
Exhibit 21: 800VDC reference design for VR
Source: Delta
Liquid cooled power shelf on the horizon
The next key check point to watch should be the Vera Rubin Ultra generation, as the
power consumption per rack shall continue to increase and there might be more hardware
devices to enable the inter-rack scale up network, both could take up more space on the
IT rack. That said, the power shelf will shift from air cooling into liquid cooling, which

could narrow the power shelf height by 1U to free up some space as well. Our checks
indicate that both the in-rack power shelf and 800VDC power rack solutions are ongoing
now, and we believe that rack space, power redundancy and system reliability are all the
factors being considered. We'll closely monitor developments on this front.
Exhibit 22: Liquid cooled power shelf
Source: Megmeet
Exhibit 23: Liquid cooled power shelf in rack
Source: Megmeet

Solid state transformers on the Nvidia RVL for the first time
In the MGX ecosystem board, where Nvidia usually list the recommenced vendor lists and
product samples, we saw SST this time. Delta, Heron. DG Matrax, Sungrow, Solaredge,
Megmeet, Luxshare, etc.

### Page 12

M
Idea
12
Exhibit 24: SST vendors on the Nvidia MGX ecosystem board
Source: Morgan Stanley
Looking further ahead, Solid-State Transformers (SSTs) represent a potential next-
generation evolution of power infrastructure. Unlike traditional transformers that operate
primarily as passive devices, SSTs leverage power electronics to provide voltage
conversion, power conditioning, and bidirectional energy management within a single
platform. This enables greater controllability and flexibility while supporting increasingly

digitalized power networks.
Although SSTs remain at an early stage of commercialization and face challenges related
to economics, reliability, and large-scale deployment, the technology aligns well with long-
term data center trends. As AI campuses evolve into utility-scale power consumers
incorporating energy storage, distributed generation, and advanced power management,
SSTs could eventually become a key enabling technology.

### Page 13

M
Idea
Morgan Stanley Research 13
Therefore, while HVDC is expected to drive the next phase of power architecture
upgrades, SSTs represent a longer-term technology roadmap that could further transform
how future data centers distribute and manage electricity.

According to Infineon, SST could represent an emerging >$1bn market opportunity by
2030, as the technology begins to replace a portion of the >$15bn conventional
transformer market, particularly in smaller-power applications. While SST penetration is
expected to remain relatively modest versus the overall transformer market, the
opportunity is significant for relevant players in the longer term.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
Lite-On Technology (2301.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 17x based on average 2027-28 EPS. We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 47% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.4, versus
the stock's 16x P/E and 2% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 7.2.
Risks to Upside
n Stronger optical business growth
n Stronger data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand growth

n Downtrend in raw material prices
Risks to Downside
n Macro demand weakness
n Weaker data center/server demand
n PC/NB demand slowdown
n Uptrend in raw material prices
Delta Electronics Inc. (2308.TW)
Base case: P/E multiple of 30x based on average 2027-28 EPS.                          We believe our target multiple
is justified by the 63% earnings CAGR we estimate for 2025-28, implying a PEG of 0.5, versus
the stock's ~23x P/E and 23% earnings CAGR during 2022-25, which implied a PEG of 1.0.

Risks to Upside
n Faster-than-expected global economic growth
n Falling raw material prices
n Margin improvement
Risks to Downside
n Global economic slowdown
n Ongoing component supply tightness
n Rising raw material prices
n Margin contraction as a result of higher operating expenses or faster ASP erosion
Chroma Ate Inc. (2360.TW)
Base case, 45x 2027e P/E. We use P/E as our valuation methodology because we think it better
captures Chroma's earnings growth momentum. The stock traded at an average valuation of

~19x P/E during 2014-25 with an operating profit CAGR of 20%, implying a PEG of 1.0. We think
our target multiple of 45x is justified by the 61% operating profit CAGR we project for
2025-28.
Risks to Upside
n Better-than-expected macro conditions
n Stronger semi and photonics and power testing demand

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

公司簡介 
矽力杰為電源管理晶片大廠，產品線包括
DC/DC 轉換晶片、電池充電管理晶片、過
流保護晶片、靜電防護裝置與 LED 背光驅
動晶片、能源監控管理晶片等。2Q26 產品
終端應用主要區分為四大領域 ， 包括消費電
子 36%、工業應用 35%、資訊產品 9%與

王文雯 wenwen.wang@sinopac.com 
本次報告：電話訪談

矽力*-KY (6415 TT) 
資料中心與車載為成長主軸

永豐觀點 
矽力杰 3Q26 營收優於預期，4Q26 展望正向，雖短期毛利率較為波動，不過隨資
料中心與車載訂單加持，以及第四代製程平滲透率提升， 應有助於中長期毛利率回
穩。 
 投資評價與建議 
維持買進：研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年稅後 EPS 11.95 元(原預估 11.59 元)，2027
年稅後 EPS 15.34 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營運拓展，研究部
維持買進建議，目標價維持 644 元(42X 2027 年 EPS 評價)。

ESG 評析 
矽力*-KY 企業永續評鑑整體為 B 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面
向平均得分分別為 B+等與 B 等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

資料來源：SinoPac+ ESG 評等 (依循 2023 SinoPac+企業永續評鑑方法學) 
註 1：資誠永續發展服務股份有限公司僅於ＥＳＧ評鑑系統方法學建置過程中，就評分指標提供
專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。 
註 2：永豐投顧已獲 SASB 授權，於研究中使用 SASB 重大性地圖之一般議題分類及揭露主題。
SinoPac Securities Investment Service licenses and applies the SASB Materiality Map® 
General Issue Categories and Disclosure Topics in our work.

### Page 2

2

矽力杰為電源管理晶片大廠：矽力杰成立於 02/2008，為電源管理 IC 設計公司，
產品線包括 DC/DC 轉換晶片、電池充電管理晶片、過流保護晶片、靜電防護裝置
與 LED 背光驅動晶片、能源監控管理晶片等。矽力杰 2Q26 產品終端應用主要區
分為四大領域，包括消費電子(如平板電腦、STB 與 TV)36%、工業應用(如 LED 照
明、伺服器與安防設備等)35%、資訊產品(如筆電)9%與網路通訊(如路由器、手機
等)6%、 車載14%。國際電源管理晶片設計同業與矽力杰產品類似者為 TI、Maxim、
ON Semi 與 ADI，台系類似的參與廠商則包括致新、茂達等。 
3Q26 營收優於預期 ，4Q26 展望正向 ：矽力杰總計 3Q26 營收 72.83 億元
(+21%QoQ，+53%YoY)，落於研究部原預估 3Q26 營收達成率 102%(研究部原
預估季增 18%)，優於預期，主要係由資料中心為最主要推升力道，再者車載因新
款晶片推出與市占率提升而增 、 消費電子即便市場需求較為疲弱， 但因公司新款晶
片推出，故反而仍能向上。目前展望 4Q26 亦維持正向看法，營收變化主要仍需視
後段封測產能的供給程度而定。研究部預估矽力杰 3Q26 毛利率 47.63%，稅後獲
利 11.32 億元(-28%QoQ，+66%YoY)，稅後 EPS 2.9 元，並預估 4Q26 營收 76.9
億元(+5.5%QoQ，+43%YoY)，毛利率 47.8%，稅後獲利 12.33 億元(+9%QoQ，
+53%YoY)，稅後 EPS 3.13 元。 
主要產能瓶頸在後段封測 ：目前矽力杰最主要的產能瓶頸在後段封測的支應，主要
是後段封測供給被 AI 應用排擠，目前除原有封測廠的產能擴充外，公司策略也持
續驗證新封測廠，以其紓解封測產能緊缺問題。至於晶圓產能，則矽力杰因具備第
四代 12 吋製程平台產能(第四代製程平台滲透率由 2025 年的個位數比重 ， 已將提
升至 4Q26 的 20%)，故反而晶圓較能充足的支應，明年第五代製程平台亦將加入
量產範圍。針對 2026 年毛利率走勢仍較有變數，係因影響毛利率因子正負交錯：
正向因子包括公司依據產品與客戶別而有不等的漲價措施 、 第四代製程平台挹注與
產品結構變化，負向因子則包括晶圓/封測成本上升、存貨依據庫齡政策而有損失
或回沖認列等影響。不過研究部認為隨 07/01 已開始進行漲價，理應能消弭部分
製造成本攀升的衝擊，遂預估 2H26 毛利率應能優於 2Q26 水準，中長期隨第四
代製程平台滲透率提升與產品結構變化， 理應有助於毛利率趨勢向上。 相較毛利率
是否獲得顯著提升，目前公司更關心在希冀產能能獲得更充足的支援， 以滿足客戶
的需求。 
資料中心與車載為後續成長主軸：資料中心、車載與工業將成為推升後續成長的主
軸：(1) 資料中心(包括主板/企業級 SSD/光模組)目前營收比重 15%~16%，2027
年 目 前 維 持 預 期>20% 比 重 ， 公 司資 料 中 心 三 大 項 目 比 重 依 序 為光 通 訊
>SSD>MB，其中光通訊與 SSD 個別均為 mid-to-high single digit，MB 比重較
低。公司過往以 400G 以下為主，今年 400G/800G 均已導入，隨市占率逐步提
升，又未來若主板核心電源出貨，更將進一步推動資料中心業務規模擴大。公司亦
表示 PSU 相關產品出貨亦隨伺服器需求而增，公司 PSU 產品包括 AC-DC 
controller/ESD/Boost/ Buck/Temp sensor等，目前針對 VPD 進展還沒那麼快。
(2) 車載營收比重可望由去年的 13%， 攀升至今年的15%~20%， 研究部認為車載
動力來自於公司車載晶片供應品項增加， 每台車的可供應的內含價值擴大， 並且客
戶群攀升，除中國品牌上量外，也進一步拓展海外市場。(3) 儲能與光伏需求，以
及充電樁應用，亦能拓展工業領域的需求。 
維持買進： 研究部預估矽力杰 2026 年營收 258.35 億元(+37%YoY)，毛利率
47.65%，稅後獲利 47.05 億元(+90%YoY)，稅後 EPS 11.95 元(原預估 11.59 元)，
並預估 2027 年營收 341.9 億元(+32%YoY)，毛利率 48.44%，稅後獲利 60.39 億
元(+28%YoY)，稅後 EPS 15.34 元。考量矽力杰資料中心與車載仍將持續帶動營

營運現況與分析

SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統級距說明 
A+ 企業在管理及揭露 ESG 績效的程度在前 20% 
A 企業在管理及揭露 ESG 績效的程度在 21%-40% 
B+ 企業在管理及揭露 ESG 績效的程度在 41%-60% 
B 企業在管理及揭露 ESG 績效的程度在 61%-80% 
C 企業在管理及揭露 ESG 績效的程度在 81%-100%

1 
6426 統新 
(Apogee Optocom Co.)

報告日期： 2026/10/19 研 究 員：方姿婷  (Lesley Fang) 
壹、投 資 建 議 
重點摘要： 
 統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率
躍升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。
2Q26 受惠生產效率提升、產能滿載毛利率躍升。 
 AI 伺服器帶動統新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要
骨幹濾片，光纖波段分離與光纖傳輸頻寬增加，會需要統新薄
膜濾光片作為訊號傳輸強化。除 Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分
散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，
2026~2031 年每年維持 31%年複合成長。 
 1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD，會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光
片。

一. 統新 2Q26 營收與財報優於市場預期，3Q26 續強 
統新 2Q26 營收為 2.08 億元，+34.7%QoQ、+82.4%YoY，毛利率躍
升至 56.20%，稅後淨利 0.86 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.22 元。主要受
惠於 AI 資料中心互連需求，使得統新產線維持滿載。展望 3Q26
預估統新營收為 2.42 億元，+16.35%QoQ，毛利率 56.58%，稅後淨
利 1.06 億元，單季稅後 EPS+2.76 元。統新預計產能增加 30%，以
因應客戶需求。

二. 2026 年 DWDM 隨 AI 骨幹網絡建置，濾片量增價漲 
統新高密度波長多工器( D W D M，D e n s e  W a v e l e n g t h  D i v i s i o n  
Multiplexing)，至少可傳輸 16 種不同顏色的光纖，最高可傳輸 128
種不同顏色之光纖，同一條主幹網路上，可以傳輸的頻寬因此加
大，相當具有傳輸效率。DWDM 於發送端將不同波長的光信號組
合起來(覆用, MUX)，並耦合到光纜線路上的同一根光纖中，進行
傳輸，在接收端又將多個波長的光信號分開(解覆用, DEMUX)，並
作進一步處理，恢復出原波長信號後送入不同的終端接收器。統
新的 DWDM 產品延伸至 AI 資料中心 DCI 主要骨幹濾片，波段分
離 ( S -B a n d、C - B a n d 傳 輸 核 心、L -B a nd 容 量 擴 充、S -B a nd 未 來 預 留、
U-Band 8nm 用於 OTDR 光纖監控偵測主幹網路是否具有斷點或需
要救援)，統新以單通道 100GHz 去細分 DWDM 濾光片，統新推出
新品，50GHz 間隔濾光片，支援的通道數量提升至 96Channels。 
投資評等：買進 
目標價位：375.00 元

三.  AI 伺服器高速傳輸，帶動光通訊主被動元件量增 
Scale-Across 長距離骨幹，分散式 AI 數據中心，專門做長距離光
通訊傳輸 DWDM 市場，2026~2031 年，DWDM 市場每年維持 31%
年 複合 成長。此 外，受惠 AI 伺服 器傳 輸速 率提升，如：Broadcom、
Marvell 等國際晶片廠商，於 2026 年起已推出 800G /1.6T Switch 晶
片組，等同於 AI 伺服器後端 Ports 支援的光纖通訊傳輸會一併升
級至 800G 與 1.6T 傳輸速率。Yole 報告指出，1.6T 光纖收發模組
出貨量，2026 年/2027 年/2028 年預估量 15~20M/55~60M/85~100M，
1.6T 光纖收發模組 ， 未來 2 年內呈倍數增長 。 1.6T 光纖收發模組，
在後端 PD(接收端)改採 200G PD ， 會用到 8 片統新之薄膜濾光片。
在薄膜濾光片鍍膜層數 ， Scale-Across 與 NPO 所需薄膜濾光片層數
約 150L~200L，未來發展至 CPO 時鍍膜層數會再增加 8L，統新為
此預計多準備 10 條產線預計開發 250L+規格，予台系知名晶圓代
工廠商。統新指出，Scale-Out 濾片鍍膜代工量增加，是替中系系
統廠代工(層數約 100~150L)。Scale-Across 濾片鍍膜層數會大於
200L，甚至高達 250L，目前訂單能見度高，涵蓋 1.)同業產能難以
支應，Cohr 交給統新、2.)中系系統廠商加單、3.)台系網通廠商於
低軌衛星鍍膜(LEO 非光學鍍膜) 。統新毛利率之所以跳升，是先前
客戶給統新的試產量僅 20~30 片，2Q26 起量產量增至 K 級別，出
貨量增加、規模經濟效應之下，單月營收損平點(評估為 0.4~0.5
億元)，2Q26 起月營收顯著跳升至 0.6~0.7 億元。預期此營運動能
至少能見度到 4Q26，但由於 DWDM 交期延長至 6~8 個月，預估
2027 年產業供需偏向緊俏。

四.投資建議：買進，目標價 375 元 
統新競爭優勢在於其生產高品質高效率之薄膜濾光片篩選特定輻
射波段的光學元件，能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動
截面，藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中 
傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高光纖的使用效率，生產良率逐步提
升且具佔有一定之市場地位。預估統新 2026 年營收 8.56 億元，
+75.77%YoY，毛利率 53.43%，稅後淨利 3.32 億元，稅後 EPS 為
+8.63 元。展望 2027 年，隨產能積極擴充之下，預估統新年營收
為 17.02 億元，+98.83%YoY ，毛利率預估為 54.85% ，稅後淨利 5.57
億元，稅後 EPS+14.47 元。考量基礎建設帶動 DWDM 規格升級，
AI 伺服器傳輸速率提升因應 1.6T 傳輸薄膜濾光片用量同步增長，
給予統新投資建議買進，目標價 375 元(26.00 X2027 EPS)。

一.公司簡介

統新(6426)為生產精密光學元件，以及高密度分波多工器(DWDM)
關鍵元件─薄膜濾光片(TFF)之專業廠商。各產品線營收占比為主
動光學元件佔比低於 15%、被動光學元件：80~85%、光學鍍膜元
件與代工低於 10%。主要產品 DWDM 濾光片 (Dense Wavelength 
Division Multiplexer，即為高密度波長多工分工器)技術可以大幅提
高光纖通訊系統之頻寬，成為光纖通訊系統的市場中關注的焦
點。統新國內主要競爭對手為東典光電科技(股)，國外廠商包含
II-IV Cohernet(美國)、Iridian Technologies(加拿大)等廠商投入相關
產品研發及生產。

圖一、光通訊元件

資料來源：公司、宏遠投顧整理

二.26 年營運概況及 26 年展望 
1.統新 DWDM 技術層層堆疊良率優異  
統新主要產品為薄膜濾光片即為篩選特定輻射波段的光學元件，
能篩選光的強度、改變光譜成分或限定震動截面，藉由不同光線
原理而選擇通過的光源(紅、藍、綠光) ，且藉由 CWDM/DWDM 技
術使相同數據集中於同一根光纖中傳輸，增大傳輸容量進而提高
光纖的使用效率。

2.因應 AI 資料中心基建商機，薄膜濾光片產品走向更高規  
回顧 5G 時期的薄膜濾光片，支援頻段增加，切割更細，中心波

長 1267.5nm、1274.5nm 切割更為細窄，至 1374.5nm 從過往支援
波長 6 組延伸至 12 組 ， 每一道光波段間距縮短至 3.5nm 甚至更
短，薄膜濾波濾光片用量增加，設計難度提升。隨著支援頻段愈
切分愈細，波段之間間距愈來愈短，需要用的薄膜濾波片數量倍
增，且每一片薄膜濾波片設計難度提升，包含設計層數 200 層以
上以及過濾效果較佳，而統新於濾波片設計良率優於競爭同業，
可望取得一定市場地位。

seohyun.woo@meritz.co.kr 3Q26 OP 80.1조원 실적 서프라이즈 예상  
3Q26 영업이익: 상반기와 다르다. 억제 요인들의 추세 반전 전환 
SK하이닉스의 3Q26 영업이익은 전분기 대비 32% 증가한 80.1조원으로 시장기대
치를 능가할 전망이다. 최근 원화강세 흐름 및 상반기 동사의 상대적 실적개선 속도 
열위에 3Q26 영업이익 추정치는 70조원까지 내려갔다 (컨센서스 74조원). 하지만 
이제 동사 출하량 및 판가의 탄력적 복원세가 경쟁사 보다 빠른 실적 개선을 이끌 전
망이다. 3Q26 DRAM과 NAND 출하량/판가 증감률은 각각 +13%/+22%, 
+4%/+15% 수준으로, 각 영업이익은 63조원/17조원으로 예상된다 (OPM 각각 
84%/69% 추정). 그간의 상대적 실적 열위는 이제 반전된다. 이는 1) 판가 전략에 
있어 중장기적 관점에서 고객 중심 유연성 극대화를 꾀했던 부분의 보상적 되돌림이 
발생하고, 2) 업계에서 제일 높은 HBM 판매 비중으로 인해 판가 상승률에 발목 잡혔
지만, 오히려 HBM4 본격 공급이 시작되며 이익기여가 본격화된다. 아울러 3) 모바
일 핵심고객과의 기성 계약이 산업 전반적 LTA 논의로 재검토되며 공급사에 크게 유
리한 조건으로 변화되어 가기 시작한다. 당사는 1Q27부터 모바일 메모리 판가 급등
을 전망하며 (그림1), 2026E/2027E 영업이익의 경우 264조원/454조원으로 상향조정한
다. 4Q26 내 시장 실적 추정치 상향조정 및 투자자 수급의 구조적 개선을 전망한다.

‘지능기술’ 시대, 수요의 가격 역탄력성 속 중장기 기회창출에 주목 
과거 B2C 사이클과 다르게, 금번 B2B 사이클은 수요의 가격 역탄력성을 보이고 있
다. AGI 도달을 위한 선착순 투자가 핵심이기 때문이다. 빅테크 중 낙오자가 발생하
기 전까지 경쟁적 대규모 투자는 지속된다. 최근 동사의 솔리다임 상장 추진보도에 
일부 우려가 제기되고 있지만, 투자자들은 그 이면의 중장기적 기회에 주목해야 한다. 
동사는 LTA 외에도, 다양한 파트너십을 추진하며 중장기 시장 예측력을 증가시키고 
있다. 솔리다임 상장 과정에서 유입되는 현금은 모회사 ‘SK AI co’의 지분교환 및 JV 
구축 등에 활용되리라 예상된다. 이는 중장기 수요를 ‘창출’시키는 개념이다.

순수한 주주가치 제고 목적 환원 재도출 예상. 적정주가 상향조정 
빠르게 증가한 현금흐름 중 ‘잉여’분은 주주가치 ‘회복’에 우선 배분돼야 함이 마땅하
며, 동사 역시 최근 공시를 통해 주주가치 제고와 주가의 저평가 인식을 드러냈다. 현
재 진행중인 40조원 자사주 매입 프로그램 종료 후 즉각적인 신규 환원 정책 도출이 
예상된다. 2027년 메모리 공급부족은 명확하다. 역사적으로 효율적 시장 내, 주가 조
정은 탄력적 복원으로 귀결되어왔다. 동사에 대한 투자의견 Buy를 유지하며, 적정주
가의 경우 320만원으로 상향한다 (26E/27E 평균 P/B 4.0배 적용). 
 Meritz Research  2026. 10. 12

Buy (20거래일 평균종가 대비 상승 여력 기준)

## Document 225: AIの雇用への影響をどう読むか

Source PDF: all report pdf/AIの雇用への影響をどう読むか.pdf
Report source: DIR
Date: 2026-10-14
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

米国

株式会社大和総研 丸の内オフィス 〒100-6756 東京都千代田区丸の内一丁目 9 番 1 号 グラントウキョウ ノースタワー 
このレポートは投資勧誘を意図して提供するものではありません。このレポートの掲載情報は信頼できると考えられる情報源から作成しておりますが、その正確性、完全
性を保証するものではありません。また、記載された意見や予測等は作成時点のものであり今後予告なく変更されることがあります。㈱大和総研と大和証券㈱は、㈱大和
証券グループ本社を親会社とする大和証券グループの会社です。内容に関する一切の権利は㈱大和総研にあります。無断での複製・転載・転送等はご遠慮ください。

2026 年 10 月 14 日 全13 頁

AI の雇用への影響をどう読むか 
一部の職種で狭まる若手の「入口」、経験を積む場をどう残すか

AI アナリティックリサーチ室 主任研究員 新田 尭之

[要約] 
 近年の実証研究では、米国を中心に、生成AI の影響を受けやすい一部の職種で若手の
雇用悪化が相次いで報告されている。悪化は主に採用の減少として表れており、最初
の職を得る「入口」が狭まっている。ただし、悪化の一部は ChatGPT 公開前に始まっ
ており、在宅勤務の広がりやコロナ禍の採用増の反動とも重なる。複数の要因が考え
られる中、AI の影響の大きさについて明確な結論は得られていない。 
 若手の「入口」が狭まる一方、米国全体では雇用者数が増えている。もっとも、 雇用
増の多くは民間教育・医療によるもので、幅広い産業には広がっていない。採用も離
職も少ないため、既に職に就いている人は職を失いにくいが、これから職を得る人や
転職を望む人にとっては空席が少ない。さらに、AI による省力化は、人員を減らすよ
り、業務の中身や外注を見直す形で進む例が多い。雇用者数だけでは、こうした仕事
の機会の変化は見えにくい。 
 今後の雇用を分けるのは、AI による値下げや品質向上で需要が広がったり、新しい業
務が生まれたりして、どれだけ人手が必要になるかである。ただし、新たな仕事が生
まれても、若手に回るとは限らない。41 カ国の多国籍企業の分析では、生成AI を導入
した企業の拠点で経験者の雇用が増え、若手の比率が下がった。経験者の多くは、入
口の仕事を通じて育つ。若手の採用を絞る判断は、個々の企業には合理的でも、続け
ば将来の経験者が育たない「育成の空洞化」を招きかねない。若手が経験を積む場を
どう残すかが問われており、社内で人を育てる日本企業も無縁ではいられないだろう。

1. はじめに 
近年、生成 AI の進化や普及を背景に、AI が雇用に与える影響について、米国を中心に実証
研究が蓄積されている（図表1）。 
図表 1 生成 AI が雇用に与える影響に関する近年の主な実証研究

職種。 
（出所）各研究より大和総研作成

### Page 3

1

3 / 13

例えば、スタンフォード大学の研究者らは、給与計算・人事サービスを手掛ける ADP の給与
記録を用いて米国の労働市場を分析し、主に次の 6 点を報告している 1。①AI による経済全体
にわたる広範な雇用喪失は確認されない。②一方、AI の影響を受けやすい職種では、受けにく
い職種に比べて22～25 歳の雇用の伸びが弱く、年長層には同程度の差はみられない。③この若
年層の職種間格差は2026 年半ばにかけて拡大を続けた。④その差は主に離職の増加ではなく、
採用の減少による。⑤雇用の減少はAI が人の作業を代替する使い方の多い職種に集中し、仕事
を補完する使い方では雇用の横ばい・増加との関連がみられる。 ⑥調整は基本給よりも雇用の
面に表れている。 
こうした結果は、AI が雇用に与える影響を、全体の人数だけでなく、年齢や採用・離職、AI
の使われ方などに分けて捉える必要性を示している。そこで、本稿ではこうした実証研究や統
計データを基に、①雇用の悪化が目立つ層と、そのうちAI の影響と読める範囲（第 2 章） 、②
雇用者数だけでは捉えにくい仕事の機会の変化（第3 章 ）、 ③今後の雇用を分ける要因（第4 章）
を検討する。

2. 悪化は「入口」で目立つが、AI だけでは説明できない 
（1）AI の影響を受けやすい職種の若年層を中心に悪化 
近年の実証研究を見ると、米国の若年層で雇用の悪化が目立つ。前述のスタンフォード大学
の研究では、22～25 歳の雇用者数は2026 年6 月にChatGPT 公開時の2022 年11 月を約2％下回
った一方、26 歳以上の各年齢層では増加し、特に35～40 歳では約11％多い（図表2）。 
図表 2 年齢層別に見た雇用者数の推移（米国、2022 年 11 月＝1）

（注）ADP の給与記録のうち、2021 年 1 月～2026 年 6 月に継続して観測される企業の 70 歳未満のフルタイム労
働者（毎月 350 万～500 万人） 。年齢層別人口の変化は未調整。 
（出所）Brynjolfsson, Chandar, and Chen [2026]より大和総研作成（画像の加工・仮訳は大和総研）

1 Brynjolfsson, Chandar, and Chen [2026]. AI 曝露はEloundou et al. [2024] の指標のうち GPT-4 による
判定の値で、職業を五つの群に分けて比べている。

### Page 4

1

4 / 13

ただし、若年層の雇用が職種を問わず悪化したわけではない。同研究の職種別の推計では、
AI への曝露（仕事の内容がAI で代替・支援されうる度合い）が最も高い職種群の22～25 歳の
雇用の伸びは、2022 年11 月から2026 年6 月までに、最も低い職種群の雇用の伸びを約18％ポ
イント下回った。若年層の中でも、AI の影響を受けやすい職種で悪化が目立つ。 
さらに、米国国勢調査局の研究者らは、大学の卒業記録と雇用の行政記録を 接続し、専攻別
に卒業生の就職状況を比べた。これによると、 AI への曝露が最も高い専攻の卒業生は、
ChatGPT 公開直後から、卒業翌四半期に就業している割合が曝露の低い専攻の卒業生と比べて
低下し始め、2024 年以降は約5％ポイント低い2。 
米国の約6,500 万人分の職歴データを企業単位で分析した研究でも、生成AI の導入企業では
若手（職位の低い層）の雇用が2023 年初めから6 四半期で比較対象の企業より約9％減った。
これも主に採用の減少によるという 3。求人の側では、求人サイト Indeed の分析で、テック職
で 5 年以上の経験を求める割合の上昇が確認されている4。 
ニューヨーク連銀が集計したデータからも、若年大卒者の悪化は確認できる。22～27 歳の若
年大卒者の失業率は、2018 年までほぼ一貫して全労働者を下回っていたが、2019 年以降はおお
むね上回るようになり、2026 年 6 月は 5.7％と全労働者（4.1％）を 1.6％ポイント上回る。大
卒でない若年層（22～27 歳）の失業率が 2025 年秋を頂点に下がり始めた後も、若年大卒者の
失業率は高止まりしている（図表3）5。 
図表 3 全労働者と若年層の失業率（米国）

（注）季節調整値の3 か月移動平均。全労働者は16～65 歳、大卒でない若年層は22～27 歳の学士未満、若年大
卒者は 22～27 歳の学士以上で、いずれも在学中の人を除く。 
（出所）ニューヨーク連銀 “The Labor Market for Recent College Graduates”（2026 年8 月6 日）

5 / 13

若年大卒者の失業率を専攻別に見ると、2024 年の失業率はコンピューター工学が 7.8％、コ
ンピューター科学が 7.0％と、全専攻（4.2％）を大きく上回り、73 専攻の上位 5 位以内に入
る。一方、就職後の年収中央値は 9 万ドル、8.7 万ドルと全専攻（5.8 万ドル）を大きく上回
り、大卒資格を要しない職に就く不完全就業率も低い。就業者の年収水準の高さと、失業率の
高さが併存している（図表4）。 
図表 4 専攻別に見た若年大卒者の失業率と年収中央値（米国、2024 年）

（注）失業率は 22～27 歳の学士以上の卒業生（在学中の人を除く） 、年収中央値は22～27 歳の学士のみのフル
タイム就業者。赤はコンピューター系の専攻。 
（出所）ニューヨーク連銀 “The Labor Market for Recent College Graduates”（2026 年8 月6 日） 
 より大和総研作成

なお、オンラインのフリーランス市場でも、AI の影響を受ける分野では、補完的とみなされ
る技能の分野を含め、経験の浅い人向けの案件が、影響を受けない分野に比べて相対的に減っ
たと報告されている 6。年齢と経験の浅さは同じ区分ではないが、若手の 雇用悪化と経験の浅
い人向けの案件の減少は、最初の仕事を得る機会、いわば「入口」が狭まっている点で共通す
る。 
（2）ChatGPT 公開前からの悪化や在宅勤務の広がりなども重なる 
ただし、こうした悪化の原因すべてを生成AI の影響に帰するのは早計であろう。第一に、悪
化の一部は ChatGPT 公開前に始まっていた。例えば、米国のソフトウェア開発職の求人は、
2022 年 2 月をピークに急減し、ChatGPT 公開時には既にピークを3 分の 1 あまり下回っていた
（図表5）。 
ニューヨーク連銀のブログに掲載された求人データの分析でも、AI 曝露の高い職種の求人の
相対的な減少は2022 年より前に始まっており、2022 年以降に傾向の明確な変化は見られない。

6 Teutloff et al. [2025]. 受注・収入の減少はHui, Reshef, and Zhou [2024] も参照。

### Page 6

1

6 / 13

同じ分析では、高曝露職の中で若手（職位の低い層）向けと経験者向けの求人がほぼ並行して
動いており、求人の減少は入口の仕事に集中していないという 7。 
図表 5 ソフトウェア開発職の求人指数（米国）

（出所）Indeed Hiring Lab より大和総研作成

第二に、在宅勤務の広がりである。ニューヨーク連銀のブログに掲載された分析によると、
米国の在宅勤務が可能な職業では、若手と年長の大卒者の失業率の差が2019 年から 2024 年に
約 1.4％ポイント広がった 8。これは、在宅勤務が難しい職業の同期間の拡大幅（約0.5％ポイ
ント）を大きく上回る。同分析は、在宅勤務では若手への OJT や上司からの助言が難しくなる
ため、企業が経験の浅い人材の採用をためらいやすくなると指摘している。 米英加豪の採用記
録約 2.4 億件の分析でも、在宅勤務のしやすさを同時に考慮すると、AI 曝露と採用者に占める
若手（職位の低い層）の比率との関係は大きく弱まり、多くの場合、統計的に有意 な関係は確
認できなくなった 9。 
米国外の研究も、若手の雇用の減少を AI だけでは説明していない。デンマークでは、AI 曝
露の高いいくつかの職で若手の雇用が減ったものの、研究者らはその減少はAI チャットボット
を導入した企業によるものではないとしている 10。さらに、韓国銀行の研究者の分析によれば、
韓国では 2022 年 6 月から 2026 年 6 月に 15～29 歳の雇用が 28.5 万人減り、その 94％は AI の
影響を受けやすい産業で生じた 11。同研究は、コロナ禍の採用増の反動や経験者中心の採用、
在宅勤務の広がりなども作用した可能性があると指摘している。

7 Audoly, Guerin, and Topa [2026]. 求人情報などを収集・分析するLightcast の米国の求人データを用い、
AI 曝露はAnthropic が作成した、AI の実際の利用状況を反映した作業単位の指標による。若手（職位の低い
層）向け・経験者向けの区分は Lightcast が求人の記載から判定したもの。求人の数の分析であり、実際に誰
が採用されたかは捉えない点で、第 2 章（1）の研究とは対象が異なる。 
8 Emanuel, Harrington, and Pallais [2026]. 大卒者の18～28 歳と29 歳以上の失業率の差の2019 年からの
変化で、数値は同記事のチャートデータによる。 
9 Lambert and Schindler [2026]. 米英加豪の2017～2025 年のデータで、分析対象は、職名と職歴から判定し
た職位の下位 2 区分が採用者に占める比率。 
10 Humlum and Vestergaard [2026]. AI の影響を受けやすい 11 職業の約 2.5 万人への調査を2024 年 12 月まで
の行政記録と接続。新しい業務や時間の使い方は自己申告による。 
11 Oh and Oh [2026]. 雇用は国民年金の加入者数。AI の影響を受けやすい産業は、AI 曝露（Eloundou et al. 
[2024]）で全産業を 4 分位に分けた上位2 分位の産業。

### Page 7

1

7 / 13

そして先に紹介したスタンフォード大学の研究では 、在宅勤務や金利への感応度を調整して
も、22～25 歳の高曝露職と低曝露職の雇用の伸びの差は大きく変わらないが、学歴構成も含め
て調整するとその差は調整前の約 18％ポイントから約 8％ポイントに縮み、統計的に有意な関
係は確認できなくなる12。 
以上のように、一部の職種で若手の雇用が悪化していることは確認できるが、その原因を生
成 AI に求めるには慎重さが必要である。その影響を見極めるには、ChatGPT 公開前からの悪化
と、公開後に加わった可能性のある変化を分けて考える必要がある。また 、足元の弱さをコロ
ナ禍の採用増の反動だけで説明できるとは限らない。複数の要因が考えられる中で、本稿で取
り上げた研究でも、AI の影響の大きさについて明確な結論は得られていない。

3. 雇用者数だけでは捉えにくい仕事の機会の変化 
仕事を得る機会を考えるには、雇用者数が増えているかどうかに加え、どの分野で増えてい
るのか、新たな採用がどの程度行われているのかを見る必要がある。また、AI による省力化も、
人員数を変えずに業務や外注の見直しとして進む場合がある。以下では、これら三つの側面を
確認する。 
（1）雇用増は民間教育・医療に偏る 
米国では、ChatGPT が公開された2022 年 11 月から2026 年 8 月までに、情報や専門・事業サ
ービスなど、AI の影響を受けやすい職種の多い産業で雇用は減少した。一方、全体の雇用増を
支えたのは民間教育・医療である。同期間の雇用増約 480 万人のうち、約 3 分の 2（約 325 万
人）を占めた。ただし、直近 1 年の全体の雇用増は約 60 万人にとどまり、そのうち約 54 万人
を民間教育・医療が占める（図表6）。 
図表 6 業種別に見た雇用者数の増減（米国）

（注）その他は全体から掲載した業種を差し引いた残差。 
（出所）米労働統計局（BLS）より大和総研作成

12 この研究では、学歴構成は AI が仕事に影響する経路も反映している可能性があるため、その違いを調整し
て差が縮んでも、AI 以外の要因で説明できたとは限らない、としている。

### Page 8

1

8 / 13

民間教育・医療の雇用者数の伸びは、コロナ禍で落ち込んだ反動にもみえる。ただ、コロナ
禍前の2020 年2 月から見ても、雇用増約680 万人の半分（約340 万人）を占めており、雇用増
の最大の担い手であることは変わらない。全体の雇用増が、幅広い産業での雇用拡大を意味す
るわけではない。 
（2）低採用・低離職の下で就職・転職の機会は限られる 
雇用者数の動きと、新たに職を得る機会の違いは、採用と離職からも確認できる。 米国の求
人労働異動調査（JOLTS）によると、2026 年 1～7 月の採用率は平均 3.3％と、コロナ禍前の
2017～19 年（3.8％）を下回り、失業率が 7～8％台だった 2012～13 年並みに低い（図表 7）。
同じ期間の比較では、離職率も3.7％から3.3％に下がり、自己都合退職率（2.2％→2.0％）も
解雇率（1.2％→1.1％）も低下した。採用率の低さは若手だけの現象ではなく、労働市場全体
にみられる。 
図表 7 採用率と離職率（米国）

（出所）BLS より大和総研作成

離職が少なければ、欠員を埋める採用も生まれにくい。就職・転職の機会が限られる中、 先
行きへの不安から今の職にとどまる動きは「ジョブハギング（仕事にしがみつくこと） 」と呼
ばれ、2025 年 8 月ごろから米国の報道で広く取り上げられている13。米国の履歴書作成サイト
が2026 年2 月に行った調査では、自らをジョブハギングの状態にあると回答した就業者が57％
と、半年前の 45％から増えた。その 7 割は、今後半年以内に AI が自分の雇用に影響すること
を懸念していた 14。就職の入口に立つ人にとっては、いわば「空席の少ない市場」である。 
（3）AI による省力化の影響は、人員の削減より仕事の中身の見直しに表れている 
AI による省力化についても、雇用者数だけでは変化を捉えにくい。OECD が日本を含む7 カ国

13 例えば、Fortune “Workers are ‘job hugging’ in a stagnant labor market, but growing resentment 
means they could bail as soon as the next Great Resignation comes”（2025 年8 月 18 日）。 
14 ResumeBuilder.com “6 in 10 Workers Are Clinging To Their Jobs as Job Hugging Soars in 2026”
（2026 年2 月11 日）。米国の就業者2,188 人へのオンライン調査で、ジョブハギングを、会社への愛着では
なく不安から今の職にとどまることとしている。

の中小企業に行った調査では、生成 AI を使う企業の 83％が人員の必要数に影響はないと答え
た。必要な人員数の減少（9％）より、従業員の業務負担（33％）や外注への依存（14％）の
減少を挙げる企業が多い 15。社内の人員数が変わらなくても、外部に依頼する仕事は減りうる。 
前出のデンマークの研究では、時間を節約できた利用者の 85％が、その時間を他の業務に充
てると答え、雇用主が特段の施策を講じていない職場でも、利用者の8％が AI の出力の品質確
認など全く新しい業務を担っていた。研究者らは、こうした状況が、業務の見直しなど計測さ
れにくい投資が先行し、生産性の伸びが遅れて表れる「J カーブ」の初期段階と整合的だとし
ている 16。AI による省力化が、直ちに人員や労働時間の削減につながるのではなく、仕事の配
分を変える形で進んでいることがうかがえる。

4. 新たな仕事が生まれても、若手に回るとは限らない 
第 2 章で紹介した研究では、若手の雇用悪化は、主に「解雇」ではなく「採用されない」形
で表れている。この状態が続くかは、AI による省力化で生まれる余力を企業がどの業務に振り
向け、新たな需要がどれだけ人手を必要とするかにかかっている。 
省力化の後の展開は、主に三つの経路として整理できる 17。①人数を変えずに仕事の中身が
変わる。②既存の社員で対応できる範囲が広がり、採用・外注の抑制や人員削減につながる。
③値下げや品質向上による需要の拡大、新しい業務の創出が人手を必要とし、配置転換や採用
につながる。 
AI 導入後の雇用の動きには、企業規模による違いも見られる。デンマークの研究では、AI を
導入した小規模企業で新たな採用が抑えられ、雇用の増えるペースが鈍ったと報告されている。
一方、大規模企業では、同様の雇用の伸びの鈍化は確認されなかった18。 
②と③は、同じ企業の中で同時に進みうる。41 カ国の多国籍企業を対象とした研究は、AI導
入企業で増えた雇用がどの層に向かったかを示している。生成AI を導入した企業の拠点では、
ChatGPT 公開直後から若手（職位の低い層）の比率が下がり始め、2026 年 3 月には導入してい
ない企業の拠点での比率を 1.9％ポイント下回った（図表 8）19。この比率低下の主因は、若手

下の企業 5,232 社（2024 年10～12 月）。割合は生成AI を利用する企業の回答で、一時点の自己申告による。

ではない。 
17 Acemoglu and Restrepo [2019] の枠組みを基に整理した。

一致するとは限らないとしている。日本も対象に含まれるが、データが日本の雇用を捉える割合は 2％程度に
とどまり、日本単独の推計は示されていない。Hosseini Maasoum and Lichtinger [2026] は、同じ Revelio
の職歴データを用いて米国の導入企業で若手の雇用減を示しており、対象や推計方法により結果は異なる。

の雇用減少ではなく、ChatGPT 公開から約 1 年半後に始まった経験者の雇用増加である。この
研究の著者らは、AI の影響を受けやすい職種では、AI が若手の仕事を省く一方で経験者への需
要を広げたと解釈している。需要が広がっても、その担い手が若手になるとは限らないことを
示す結果である。 
図表 8 生成 AI を導入した企業の海外拠点での雇用の変化（41 カ国）

公開（2022 年 11 月） 。若手は職歴データ（Revelio Labs）の職位区分 1～2、経験者は 3～7（年齢層の区分で

③の経路を示す研究もある。米国の上場企業では、AI 投資を増やした企業ほど、主に製品の
革新を通じて売上と雇用が伸びたとされる 20。デンマークの研究で報告された、AI の出力の品
質確認などの新しい業務も③の芽といえる。 
採用の構成も、AI の使い方によって変わりうる。AI を使う既存社員が若手の入口の仕事を担
えば、採用は経験者に寄りうる。逆に、AI の支援で経験の浅い人に任せられる仕事が増えれば、

20 Babina et al. [2024]. 2010～2018 年の非テック上場企業の分析。効果は規模の大きい企業に集中する。他
社からの需要の移転も含みうるが、AI 投資の多い産業では産業全体でも売上と雇用が増えたとされる。

### Page 11

1

11 / 13

若手を採る利点は高まりうる。AI が経験の差を縮めるこうした作用は、「専門性の平準化」と
呼ばれる 21。 
もっとも、これまでの実証研究では、採用が経験者に寄る動きが目立つ。この 動きが続けば、
懸念されるのは「育成の空洞化」である。 経験者の多くは、入口の仕事を通じて育つ。若手採
用の絞り込みは、個々の企業には合理的でも、経済全体では将来の経験者層 を薄くしかねない。
AI の支援と経験者の指導を組み合わせ、若手が経験を積む場を残す工夫が求められる。 
日本企業もこの問題と無縁ではいられないだろう。日本では少子高齢化で人手不足が続き、
新卒を一括採用して社内で育てる企業が多い。このため、影響は採用数より、採る職種の構成
や、人手の足りない部署への配置転換に先に表れる可能性がある 22。とはいえ、採用数が保た
れても、AI が入口の仕事を担えば、若手が経験を積む場は細りうる。問われているのは「AI で
若手の仕事をどこまで省けるか」ではなく、 「AIを使いながら若手をどう育てるか」である。

Earnings above all 
• Higher oil prices are in a tussle with stronger growth. We expect strong growth 
to help the global economy weather inflation risks, allowing equities to focus on the 
strength of earnings growth. Oil prices remain the key risk to monitor 
• Maintain a preference for equities over bonds and cash.  Strong earnings 
growth means we would use the recent pause to add exposure to our preferred 
markets and themes. The US and Asia ex-Japan remain our preferred regions 
• Take advantage of higher bond yields via credit risk but cap duration. The 
risk of higher bond yields looks most pressing in ultra -long maturities, but credit 
quality remains strong. We are Overweight EM USD bonds 
Hits from the 1990s 
Rising bond yields have been a key market theme.  Since mid -year, 10 -year 
government bond yields have risen approximately 40-80bps across the US, Japan and 
Euro area. Against this backdrop, gold has delivered high single -digit returns, while 
global equities have delivered low single-digit returns. 
Higher oil prices are in a tussle with robust growth data . The lack of progress 
towards a negotiated settlement in the US -Iran conflict and rising fears of shipping 
restrictions extending to the Bab al-Mandab Strait have meant that oil prices are once 
again testing the USD 100/bbl level. These worries have been exacerbated by 
increasing worries about refined product (eg. diesel, jet fuel, etc) supply. Despite these 
concerns, US growth data has held up remarkably well, as illustrated by the latest PMI 
and retail sales data. This is underpinned by AI investment growth.  
Real, rather than nominal, energy prices are key.  A chart of inflation -adjusted 
energy prices, though, illustrates why the global economy has managed to hold up 
despite these risks. Oil prices in real (inflation-adjusted) terms are closer to long term 
averages rather than at true ‘oil shock’ levels. This, together with the global economy’s 
lower oil intensity relative to history, is why we expect  growth and inflation to avoid 
worst-case scenarios as long as nominal oil prices avoid an excursion into the USD 
120-150/bbl range.

The Fed is likely to hike twice more by mid -2027. The 
acceleration in AI-driven investment growth means we believe 
the chances of a shift from our core soft-landing to no-landing 
economic scenario have gone up. This, combined with still -
high inflation readings mean we now expect the Fed to hike 
rates twice more by mid -2027. In contrast, the BoJ and ECB 
are expected to hike thrice and once more respectively. 
Buy-the-dip in equities 
1990s rate hikes a better parallel then 2022 . There is 
understandably some concern about whether rising central 
bank policy rates, and rising bond yields across maturities, will 
present a challenge to equity markets. However, we believe 
rate hikes in the 1990s are a better historical parallel, when 
central banks were hiking rates into strong economic and 
earnings growth, resulting in equity markets maintaining their 
pace of gains. The 2022 hiking cycle, in contrast, represented 
an inflation surprise, which we believe is not the case today. 
Strong earnings growth overwhelms other factors today. 
Still-strong economic and earnings growth means that we 
remain constructive on the outlook for equities and other risky 
assets going into year -end and beyond. The pause over the 
last couple of months was likely driven by a combination or 
rising bond yields , September seasonality and excessive 
investor optimism around mid-year.  
However, earnings expectations for global equities remain 
very strong (especially in our preferred US and Asia ex-Japan 
regional markets). We believe these earnings remain 
sufficiently robust to outweigh the drag from higher bond 
yields. Therefore, w e remain Overweight equities relative to 
bonds and cash, though we believe it is prudent to ensure the 
size of the equities Overweight is not excessive. Within Asia, 
we also trim China equities to a core holding, leaving Taiwan 
as our only regional Overwei ght, given the ongoing focus on 
AI investment-driven markets for now. 
Bumps remain part of the journey. As we laid out in our mid-
year outlook, the second half of this year is expected to be 
characterised by positive, but bumpy returns as a result of the 
factors laid out in the previous section, but also US mid -term 
election risks. The second half has thus far played out as 
expected given sufficient bumps, but still robust earnings 
growth. We would not be surprised by some volatility around 
US mid-term elections, but any further pullbacks or pauses 
would be opportunit ies to raise exposure given strong 
earnings growth is expected to ultimately dominate. 
Prefer credit over duration 
Corporate credit quality remains strong . Aggregate 
indicators of credit quality suggest credit quality remains 
strong across both Investment Grade and High Yield bonds 
across most regions. US IG bond yields face some upward 
pressure from significant hyperscaler supply, but supply -
driven spread widening tends to be relatively short -lived in 
nature and should not signal significant credit quality worries. 
Longer-maturity bonds remain at risk of further volatility. 
In contrast, we continue to be concerned that the volatility in 
very long maturity bonds could extend. Continued inflation 
and debt worries mean it is possible investors continue to 
demand a greater ‘term premium’ (ie. a premium for holding 
bonds with very  long maturities). We continue to believe 
limiting bond duration to 3-7 years remains appropriate.  
We continue to prefer  corporate and EM over government 
bonds. This implies earning a higher yield by taking on more 
credit risk (via corporate or EM bonds) rather than adding 
more duration risk. Within bonds, we remain Overweight EM 
USD bonds and view IG and HY bonds as core holdings. 
Modest USD weakness; gold to grind higher 
USD bounce led by pricing of Fed hikes . However, we 
believe Fed hikes are now excessively priced. At the same 
time, we see room for non-US rates to rise further, especially 
in Japan where the undervalued JPY is likely to strengthen. 
This is why modest USD weakness is likely to resume. 
Gold to continue grinding higher. While the move higher is 
likely to be characteri sed by two -steps-forward-one-step-
back, we believe an ultimately softer USD and continued EM 
central bank demand should result in continued gains in the 
precious metal over both the next 3- and 12-month periods.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

With the Federal Reserve’s latest decision to raise its benchmark rate by 25 basis points in September 2026, as well as the one 
to two additional hikes predicted  before the end of the year, we are entering a new interest -rate hiking cycle. This marks an 
important shift for investors, but higher policy rates do not necessarily imply weaker portfolio returns. What matters is why rates 
are rising, how quickly they increase, and whether economic growth can absorb tighter financial conditions. 
Our framework places the current environment in a healthy/high-yield regime. Growth remains resilient, credit spreads are 
tight and the yield curve is normalising. At the same time, the economy is approaching an important boundary. Inflation remains 
above target and the 10-year US government bond yield has reached 5%. A renewed rise in inflation or a sharper slowdown in 
activity could move markets into an unhealthy/high-yield regime. 
This argues for  measured risk-taking rather than an aggressively defensive stance . We maintain a slight overweight in 
equities, hold a strong overweight in gold and a neutral allocation to fixed income. Diversification remains key near a regim e 
boundary, and hedge funds can provide useful sources of return that are less dependent on the direction of traditional markets.

Not all interest-rate hiking cycles are the same

Traditional market 
analysis often treats 
rising rates as uniformly 
negative. History 
suggests a more 
nuanced conclusion.  
We classify hiking 
cycles along two 
dimensions: whether  
the increase in rates is 
healthy or unhealthy, 
and whether it occurs  
in a high- or low-yield 
environment. 
 Fig. 8 The different regimes of interest-rate hiking cycle 
Historical interest-rate hiking cycle defined by real growth and US 10-Year treasury yields

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; Since 1974

A healthy increase in rates is generally associated with firm 
demand, above -trend growth, resilient equities and stable 
credit markets. Central banks can move gradually and remain 
data-dependent because the economy is strong enough to 
withstand tighter fin ancial conditions. An unhealthy increase 
is more likely to be driven by supply constraints or persistent 
inflationary pressures. Growth slows, credit spreads widen, 
equities de-rate and central banks are forced to react more 
aggressively. 
The second dimension is the starting level of yields. In a high-
yield environment, bonds offer meaningful income and a 
greater cushion against moderate price declines. In a low -
yield environment, that protection is limited. This distinction 
matters because the same increase in yields can have very 
different portfolio consequences depending on the income 
available at the outset.

Assessing today’s market regime: Walking the 
fine line  
We assess the current environment as healthy/high yield 
(see Fig. 9), although it is moving closer to the boundary with 
an unhealthy/high-yield regime. Growth remains resilient, with 
2026 consensus GDP growth of around 2.1%, but it is below 
its longer-term trend. Inflation has eased , but remains above 
target, with headline CPI at 3.4% year -on-year in August 
2026, leaving the Fed focused on the balance between 
persistent price pressures and a potential growth slowdown. 
Market signals over the next 6-12 months remain broadly 
supportive. The 10-year US government bond yield is around 
5%, providing the highest bond carry since 2007, while the 
yield curve is re -steepening after its prolonged inversion.  
Equities remain positive, supported by AI -related earnings 
and soft-landing expectations, and high -yield credit spreads 
are near post-global-financial-crisis lows. 
Overall, resilient markets and benign credit conditions support 
the healthy classification. However, slower growth and above-
target inflation leave little room for disappointment. Further 
disinflation would help sustain the current regime, while sticky 
inflation, a rapid rise in yields or a sharp growth slowdown 
could push it into unhealthy territory.  
Higher rates, different outcomes 
Historical returns vary significantly across the four regimes 
(see Fig. 10), but history shows that some hiking regimes 
have generated solid returns across major asset classes. 
Unhealthy regimes are more complicated. Unhealthy/high -
yield periods (top-left corner) have historically still generated 
positive average returns, but these averages can conceal 
considerable volatility and dispersion. The most damaging 
combination has been unhealthy/low yield (as in 2022), when 
traditional 60/40 portfolios declined by an average of 15.8%. 
The lesson is that the economic cause of higher rates, and the 
level of yields, matter more than the label "rate hike" alone. 
Fig. 10 Historical returns by hiking-cycle regime  
Average returns for equities, bonds, Gold and a 60% 
equity/40% bond portfolio during rate-hiking periods. 
Hiking-cycle regime 60/40  Equity  Bonds  Gold

World and global aggregate benchmarks, Standard Chartered 
Why equities can coexist with 5% bond yields 
The absolute level of bond yields does not, by itself, determine 
equity performance. Investors must also consider the 
direction, speed and underlying cause of the move. 
When yields rise gradually because growth and 
investment demand are strong, corporate earnings 
growth can offset pressure on valuation multiples.  By 
contrast, a rapid increase driven by an inflation shock or 
disorderly repricing is more difficult for equities to absorb. 
Historically, several high -yield episodes combined positive 
real growth with resilient equity markets, while inflation-driven 
episodes, such as in 2022, were more challenging. 
The current level of yields has also been substantially 
incorporated into market prices. Investors have had time to 
adjust discount rates, equity valuations and financing 
assumptions to a world in which the 10-year Treasury yield is 
around 5%. This does not eliminate risk, but it means high 
yields are no longer a wholly new shock. The greater  threat 
would be a further rapid and unexpected increase in yields. 
AI investment sits at the centre of today’s market dynamic. 
Heavy spending on data centres, semiconductors, power 
generation and digital infrastructure is supporting earnings 
and helping equities remain resilient, despite elevated yields. 
At the same time, AI-related capital demand is competing with 
rising government borrowing and bond issuance. If 
productivity gains and earnings growth ultimately justify the 
investment, higher yields and stronger equities can coex ist.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

However, if bond supply outpaces available savings or AI 
returns disappoint, yields could rise more sharply and put 
pressure on valuations. The balance between productive 
investment demand and expanding sovereign debt supply 
remains a key market theme. 
Translating the hiking-cycle regimes and 
yields level into portfolio positioning 
Our positioning reflects a healthy/high -yield regime, while 
recognising we are close to the boundary with a less 
favourable environment. Historically, this regime has 
supported positive returns across equities, bonds, gold and 
60/40 portfolios. However, pe rformance has varied 
considerably depending on whether yields were rising, stable 
or falling (see Fig. 11), reinforcing the case for selective risk -
taking and broad diversification. 
Fig. 11 Asset average returns under different high-yield 
scenarios 
Historical average performance of major asset classes 
across different path of bond yields.

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
We maintain a slight overweight in equities, focused on US 
AI-related stocks and Asia ex -Japan. Equities performed 
particularly well when high yields were stable, historically 
returning 15.1% on average, but struggled when yields rose. 
Our moderate overweight , therefore, allows participation in 
resilient growth and earnings while limiting exposure to a 
renewed rate shock. 
We hold a strong overweight in gold, which performed well 
in both rising- and stable-yield environments and delivered the 
highest return of any asset when high yields were stable. Our 
fixed-income allocation is neutral . Although today’s yields 
offer attractive income, bonds have historically been 
vulnerable when yields were rising and underperformed when 
yield were stable. 
Diversification beyond a traditional 60/40 portfolio 
remains important  at this inflection point. Hedge funds 
generated positive returns across all regimes  - rising, stable 
and falling high -yield periods  - making them a useful 
complement when the direction of rates and the path between 
healthy and unhealthy regimes is uncertain.

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): We have slightly reduced the probability of a soft landing from 50% by raising 
the odds of a ‘no -landing’. The global economy has  picked up steam this summer, powered by AI investments in the US and 
Asia. This is reflected in robust upgrades to corporate earnings estimates  and PMIs. Fiscal easing in Germany and Japan is 
supporting growth. Meanwhile, inflation has picked up amid higher energy prices due to a resurgen t Middle East conflict. As a 
result, the Fed turned hawkish, hiking rates for the first time since 2023. It was joined by the ECB and BoJ – the first time all 
three major central banks hiked rates in the same month. We expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the 
BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, disinflation should return by next year as energy and tariff effects fade, enabling 
the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. Meanwhile, China is likely to accelerate fiscal spending to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability): We raised the probability from 25% amid an acceleration in the AI-driven investment 
growth. If oil prices ease, US tax cuts, an AI -fuelled stock market boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan could boost 
“animal spirits” further. A global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also spur global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): This tail risk scenario includes 15% chance of a stagflation unfolding if geopolitical conflicts 
worsen and oil stays above USD 120/bbl. We also assign a 10% chance to a recession, potentially caused by a more rapid pace 
of rate hikes to quell inflation, a stock market downturn negating the wealth effect, or a bond selloff due to fiscal concerns.

Key chart

inflation pressures. 
 Fig. 12 More rate hikes until June 2027, before cuts in H2 2027 as inflation pressures fade 
Our policy rate outlook*; oil price and US, Euro area, German near-term, long-term inflation

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *dotted lines reflect our expected rates path over the next 12m

Policy rates watch  
Fed turns hawkish to build credibility as oil reignites 
inflation; likely to hike twice more by June 2027. The US 
economy appears resilient, with consensus estimates for 
2026 GDP clustering around 2.1% for both 2026 and 2027. 
The main engine is the AI investment boom and hyperscaler 
datacentre buildout, reinforced by easy financial conditions 
and healthy private-sector balance sheets. The robust outlook 
was reflected in August’s PMI data, with both manufacturing 
and service sector confidence beating expectations. 
The labour market has picked up  this year , with average 
monthly payrolls of 80 ,000 vs. 10 ,000 in 2025 . The 
unemployment rate at 4.1%  suggests full employment, and 
the workweek lengthened in August. Offsetting vulnerabilities 
include a low savings rate, fading fiscal support, soft real  
personal spending and oil prices close to USD 100/bbl. 
Inflation remains a challenge. Core PCE inflation has run 
above 3% every month since March, and the hawkish case 
rests on supercore inflation remaining around 3 %, raising 
concerns about energy cost pass-through. Nevertheless, 
August's firm inflation print came from airfares, hotels and a 
record jump in wireless plans  (which is idiosyncratic) , while 
shelter cooled and core goods rose just 0. 7% y/y. Core 
consumer inflation at 2. 4% y/y is the lowest since 2021, and 
BEA methodology changes in end-September should 
mechanically shave 0.2-0.3pp off historical core PCE inflation. 
Following the unanimous Fed vote to hike in September, and 
still elevated inflation, we pencil in one more rate hike this 
year, followed by another 25bps hike to 4.5% by June 2027. 
This pace is slower than market pricing of another c. 90bps of 
hikes in the next 12 months. However, we expect disinflation 
to resume next year as the impact of oil prices and tariffs fade. 
This should create space for the Fed to cut rates by Q3 2027.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 11

Global Market Outlook 11 
ECB to hike once more: Euro area activity has proved more 
resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 growth estimate 
has been revised up to 0.9%, from 0.5% as recently as July, 
with ECB staff projections upgraded to  1.4% for 2027 and 
1.5% for 2028. Manufacturing PMIs have improved to 52.7 
regionally and services PMI to 53.0, helped by German fiscal 
delivery and falling household savings rates. A softer Q3 looks 
weather-driven, due to heatwaves and low Rhine water levels, 
though higher energy prices and real yields still cap demand. 
Headline inflation hit a near three -year high of 3.3% y/y in 
August and may peak around 3.4 -3.6% in Q4 2026 before 
easing through 2027. Underlying pressures look calmer: core 
inflation drifted down to 2.4%, services eased, and negotiated 
wage growth slowed to 2.4% y/y in Q2, down from a peak of 
5.6% in 2024 . Long -term inflation expectations remain 
anchored with no evidence of second -round effects. Inflation 
risks are two -sided – oil above USD 100/bbl and gas above 
EUR 70/MWh are fuelling near -term inflation, which in turn 
should hurt demand, driving inflation below 2% by H2 2027. 
The ECB raised its deposit rate to 2.50% in September, the 
top of neutral estimates. We see scope for one more rate hike 
to 2.75% by year -end before a pause in H1 2027. As such 
pricing of 95bps more tightening toward an above-3% terminal 
rate appears too hawkish. We expect easing of energy-driven 
price pressures to allow for ECB rate cuts by Q3 2027. 
China to boost fiscal spending to support growth. China 
is entering a stabilisation phase in which production is firming, 
but the recovery remains narrow. The official manufacturing 
PMI rose to 49.8 in August from 49.2, with new orders, export 
orders and production back in expansionary territory, while 
export growth accelerated to 25.0% y/y. Momentum, however, 
is concentrated in high -tech and export manufacturing: retail 
sales, fixed-asset investment and credit growth remain soft, 
and the property downturn has yet to bottom. Full -year 2026 
growth should land near 4.5 –4.6%, broadly in line with the 
official 4.5–5% target, as domestic demand stays weak. 
Reflation is under way, but shallow and cost-driven rather than 
demand-led. Headline consumer inflation edged up to 0.8% 
y/y in August and producer inflation to 3.8%, lifted by higher 
commodity and energy prices and by official efforts to curb 
excessive price-cutting. Full-year inflation is expected around 
0.8–1.0%, against roughly zero in 2025, with producer 
inflation swinging from −2.6% to about +2.5%. The persistent 
supply-demand imbalance should cap the reflation impulse. 
Policy settings remain supportive, but with no rush to 
stimulate. Fiscal policy takes the primary role via faster 
spending and utilisation of bond proceeds, with broad fiscal 
spending potentially accelerating in H2 versus a sharp 
contraction in H1, and the augmented fiscal deficit widening 
modestly. Monetary policy plays a complementary role: a 
25bps cut in bank reserve requirements is likely, while the 
policy rate should stay unchanged. Slow public financing 
raises the likelihood of further policy fine-tuning this autumn. 
BoJ to hike once a quarter until Q2 2027. Japan's economy 
is proving more resilient than expected. The consensus 2026 
growth estimate has been revised up over the past month to 
0.8% from 0.6%. Exports rose 19% y/y in August, a twelfth 
consecutive y/y growth, while core machinery and machine 
tool orders indicate higher borrowing costs have yet to dent 
corporate investment appetite. Real wages climbed 2.4% y/y 
in July, the strongest since May 2021. The weak link is the 
household sector: spending continued to contract y/y, leaving 
an unusual two -speed economy in which external and 
corporate demand carry growth while domestic demand lags. 
Underlying inflation sits close to the 2% target, with pressure 
building upstream rather than from demand. Energy and 
commodity prices have risen sharply and earlier yen 
weakness has amplified import costs, with producer inflation 
running well ahead of CPI . The 10-year breakeven inflation 
has risen close to May’s record high of 2.3% amid growing risk 
of pass-through from producer to consumer prices.  
Bank of Japan Governor Ueda, after raising rates by 25bps to 
1.25% in September, alluded to rising inflation pressures, 
stating the policy environment has shifted to a “new phase”. 
He added that the BoJ must now act to prevent inflation from 
overshooting, rather than simply trying to reach the 2% target. 
September’s BoJ decision came in a 7-2 split vote, with two 
members nominated by Prime Minister Takaichi calling for a 
hold. However, Ueda’s post -decision comments lead us to 
believe that the BoJ board will be comfortable with a 25bps 
rate hike every quarter until Q2 2027, taking the rate to 2%.

### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12

Our view

Foundation: Fixed income assets are a  core holding (Neutral) in our portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an 
Overweight stance on EM USD government bonds and an Underweight stance on DM government bonds.  
The Fed’s decision to hike rates in the past week confirms our expectations of a central bank willing to establish its independence 
against political pressure. The Fed's moderately hawkish pivot is helping restore its inflation-fighting credentials. We expect Fed 
to hike twice, one by year-end and another hike in H1 2027 to  bring the Fed funds rate to 4.5%. The price reaction in the US 
government bond yield curve le ads us to believe the short -to-middle part of the curve ha s factored in most of the rate 
expectations. We now prefer to add positions in the 3-7-year bond maturity bucket. As oil price moderates in H2 2027, we see 
room for the Fed to contemplate rate cuts. That would also enable 10-year yields to come down, alongside the front end of the 
curve. Meanwhile, solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep yield premiums tight, even if the scope 
for further compression is limited. In EM, we continue to favour EM USD over local currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on European bank additional tier-1 (AT1) bonds (contingent convertibles [CoCos1]; FX-
hedged), US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, US utilities corporate hybrids, broad US HY and AUD corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We are Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, the 
Fed's policy pivot this month followed Chair Warsh's hawkish 
Jackson Hole speech and recent economic data prints, 
including a pick-up in job creation and inflation over the summer 
and a sharp rebound in oil prices due to the re-escalation of the 
Middle East conflict. Given the unanimous Fed decision to hike 
rates this week, we expect two more hikes by June 2027 on the 
back of robust growth amid strong AI investments and a 
balanced labour market, keeping inflation elevated.  
Over the next few months, we expect the 2-, 10- and 30-year 
US government bond yields to remain rangebound, with the 
10-year yield trading in the 5.00-5.25% range. The reasons 
for this include: 1) oil prices staying elevated and potentially 
rising further due to the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle 
East; 2) AI capex plans showing no signs of slowing down, 
which continues to drive above -trend growth in the economy 
and AI -related heavy bond issuance. While short -covering 
trades could move bond yields lower temporarily as net-short 
positions in US government bond futures have reached 
extreme levels, we think such downshifts would be short-lived. 
By H2 2027, we expect oil prices to moderate and the 
inflationary effects from tariffs to fade, allowing the Fed to 
contemplate rate cuts. US 30 -year yields, on the other hand, 
are expected to remain elevated as the US fiscal deficit 
remains substantial next year. In addition, we do not think the 
so-called ‘Bessent put’  - expanding purchases of long -dated 
bonds, while skewing new issuance towards shorter 
maturities - will be effective as this does not address the 
fundamental issues  of rising debt levels . These factors are 
likely to keep the 'term premium' elevated. 
In the Euro area and Japan, the ECB and BoJ also hiked rates 
this month. It is the first time on record all three - Fed, ECB 
and BoJ - have hiked in the same month, as they respond 
primarily to a resurgence in energy -driven inflation amid tight 
job markets. BoJ is expected to continue hiking once every 
quarter till mid -2027 as we believe the bank  is behind the 
curve and will need to speed up the pace of rate hikes.

Global Market Outlook 13 
DM corporates – Core holding  
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across developed market credit. Yield premiums have 
bounced but remain close to historical lows, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability, and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Fig. 16 AI issuance challenges IG technical backdrop 
AI issuance (total & share of total), US IG gross issuance

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion, 
particularly in the IG space, where heavy issuance related to 
AI continues to weigh on the long end of the curve. AI -driven 
supply is approaching USD 220bn for 2026  in US IG  and 
accounting for an increasingly significant share of gross 
issuance and crowding out other long-dated maturity bonds. 
We maintain a preference for selectivity at the long end, 
favouring the front to belly of the curve ( 3-7 years maturity) 
where risk -adjusted carry remains most durable. Issuance -
driven concessions in sectors with strong capital expenditure 
cycles (notably technology, capital goods, and basic 
industries) present opportunities for investors who can 
stomach near term volatility, especially when fund flows have 
remained robust: so far this year, high grade funds have seen 
cumulative inflows of ~8% of AUM. 
Fig. 17 DM IG spreads have bounced lately, but remain 
near historical lows 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
As an income proposition, DM HY continues to be well 
supported, with all-in yields in the mid -7% range and default 
rate still below trend for US HY in 2026. The shorter duration 
profile of HY, combined with its lower sensitivity to term 
premium and rate volatility, makes it an attractive sleeve in the 
current phase of the cycle. Despite experiencing net outflow 
YTD, HY demand has recovered from a net outflow position 
in the past 1-2 months, underscoring broad investor appetite 
for carry strategies even as spreads remain tight. We remain 
attentive to evolving rating migration trends and sector -
specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades slow and 
new issuance clusters around key theme s. However, we  
continue to see HY bonds as core holding allocations driven 
by healthy issuer fundamentals and stable credit metrics. 
Fig. 18 DM HY spreads are near historical lows as well 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight USD, 
core holding for local currency (LCY) bonds 
We re tain an  Overweight position in EM USD government 
bonds and retain EM LCY government bonds as a Core 
holding, reflecting a balanced perspective across both 
regional performance and currency dynamics. In EM USD 
sovereign debt, periods of spread widening have been brief  
and shallow, with attractive carry drawing sustained demand 
amid resilient global growth and stable external conditions. 
Issuance patterns remain front -loaded across many regions, 
reinforcing supportive technicals; relatively light investor 
positioning con tributes to contained risk of unwinding and 
limits market impact during episodes of moderate outflow. 
Regional nuances are increasingly notable. Latin America’s 
sovereign debt has tended to outperform, supported by more 
proactive monetary policy responses and firmer external 
balances. In Asia, higher -rated issuers offer valuable buffer 
during bouts of glob al rate volatility, while select frontier 
exposures within Africa and EMEA remain more sensitive to 
liquidity shifts and changes in risk appetite. Index composition 
also shapes resilience: the EM USD benchmark is less 
exposed to oil price shocks as non -oil importers represent a 
large share of exposure compared to the LCY universe. These 
features are particularly important as spreads across EM USD 
sovereigns hover near historically tight levels, leaving limited 
room for error. We expect returns to be primarily driven by 
carry rather than further spread compression.

### Page 15

Global Market Outlook 15

Our view

We are Overweight global equities as we expect solid earnings growth to drive the market higher . The wave of central 
bank rate hikes in September represent a near-term headwind to equity valuations, but these have been largely priced into 
markets by the rise in bond yields year-to-date. We expect earnings growth to offset valuation headwinds. We are Overweight 
US and AxJ equities, where AI adoption continues to drive growth in the technology and adjacent sectors. 
Within AxJ, we remain Overweight Taiwan, with good visibility on AI-driven growth. We lower China to a Core holding, 
as the valuation re-rating potential appears delayed. Within AxJ, we maintain core allocations to South Korea and India, and we 
are Underweight on ASEAN equities. 
We maintain a Core allocation to Japan, as nominal growth supports broad earnings revision, and a Core allocation to Europe 
ex-UK, which is enjoying an earnings inflexion. We remain Underweight UK equities, which lacks exposure to growth sectors.
