8 Equity – at a glance

Sundeep Gantori, CFA, CAIA 
Chief Investment Officer, Equities 
Fook Hien Yap 
Senior Investment Strategist 
Cindy Lam, CFA 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 16

Global Market Outlook 16

Expansion at a solid pace  
With economic activity expanding at a solid pace , we expect 
earnings growth to fundamentally drive global equities higher. 
Heading into 2027, rising AI adoption continues to drive 
strong growth  in the technology sector. Recent debates 
about regulating the pace of model developments could 
influence the mix of AI spending, with differing impacts on 
various parts of the AI value chain. However, rising adoption 
and growth in AI use cases should support massive growth in 
capex, benefitting the semiconductor industry, cloud service 
providers and internet platforms. The capex benefits also 
spillover to other industries providing the power infrastructure, 
materials and financing of the investmen ts. The risks are a 
slowdown in AI capex or net job losses from AI disruption, but 
we do not see these having a significant impact yet. 
Rate hikes  from central banks represent a headwind for 
equity valuations, although this has been largely priced in by 
bond markets, given the rise in yields we have seen this year. 
Further rate hikes are being priced by the market. However, 
we expect these headwinds to be offset by solid earnings 
growth in global equities: at 34% in 2026 and 16% in 2027.  
Fig. 21 Global equities continue to be supported by 
strong earnings growth, led by the technology sector  
Earnings growth by sectors for MSCI AC World index

Source: Company reports, Standard Chartered  
US equities – Overweight  
US equity valuations have softened this year  as strong 
earnings growth have outpaced the rise in the market. The 
12m forward P/E of around 20x remains at a premium to other 
markets but justified, in our view, by the high returns on equity, 
differences in sector composition and superior growth outlook. 
In the run-up to US mid-term elections in November, we would 
not be surprised to see higher volatility, though we would use 
any undue corrections as opportunities to add exposure. 
AxJ equities – Overweight 
AxJ enjoys the strongest earnings growth among major 
markets, as its technology sector remains a key 
beneficiary of the AI capex cycle. AxJ also has the lowest 
P/E valuation, given the earnings surge ha s not yet been  
reflected in the market. Within AxJ, we are Overweight 
Taiwan, which benefits from clear visibility on AI -driven 
growth and dominance in manufacturing advanced AI chips. 
We lower China to a Core holding as the valuation re -
rating potential now appears delayed. Share placements for 
AI spending will likely continue, diluting earnings. Regulatory 
concerns still linger from US restrictions on AI chips and 
distillation accusations, even as the Trump-Xi summit signals 
a mutual desire for steady ties. Looming offshore trust taxes 
may lead to further sell down pressures , while domestic 
demand remains weak. However, valuation s remain 
inexpensive with rapid tech innovation and AI -localisation 
investments. Policy stimulus could provide upside. 
South Korea equities remain a Core holding. We are 
positive on the long-term agreements giving greater earnings 
visibility on memory chip s. Positioning is also cleaner, with 
reduced foreign investor exposure and declin ing leveraged 
ETFs. India remains a Core holding amid positive earnings 
revision and strong domestic demand , alongside  macro 
headwinds from RBI rate hikes. We remain Underweight on 
ASEAN, as oil shocks and a supported USD could weigh on 
the region, although Singapore offers defensive growth. 
Fig. 22 Global equity valuations have declined this year, 
with market gains led by earnings growth 
Composition of returns YTD for regional MSCI equity indices

Source: FactSet, Standard Chartered. Min-Max over last 10 years 
Japan equities – Core allocation 
Rising nominal economic growth is supporting solid 
broad-based earnings revisions , with rising rates 
supportive of bank earnings. The fiscal support for strategic 
industries is also pro-growth, offsetting energy shocks.  
Europe ex-UK equities – Core allocation  
Europe ex-UK is seeing an earnings inflection  after three 
stagnant years, with rising rates supportive of banks’ 
earnings. Fiscal expansion is benefitting domestic demand 
and the industrial sector, although earnings growth in the 
region still lags that of the US and AxJ.  
UK equities – Underweight  
The UK lacks exposure to growth sectors, with relatively 
muted earnings growth. We expect this to weigh on the UK , 
making it lag the performance of other markets.

Global Market Outlook 17

Add Global Semiconductors, remove HSTECH 
We initiate d an Opportunistic Idea on Global 
Semiconductors on 9 Sep t 2026. We expect sustained AI 
investments and rising AI adoption to support earnings 
growth, with demand widening beyond processors to memory, 
networking, and advanced manufacturing equipment . 
Valuations have also eased since the middle of the year. 
We remove Hang Seng Technology (31 Oct 2024 to 24 Sep 
2026: -1.2%) amid mixed earnings trends, while AI 
monetisation has yet to keep pace with rising investments. 
Widening US-China tech restrictions adds to the uncertainty, 
and we see better risk-adjusted opportunities elsewhere.

Ongoing Opportunistic Ideas 
Euro area banks: A higher-for-longer rates backdrop should 
extend the earnings upcycle, with further ECB tightening  
expected. Corporate loan demand is showing recovery, while 
non-interest income  is growing . Strong capital generation 
supports higher dividends and continued share buybacks. 
Global Power & Electrification: Rising AI and data centre 
power demand should drive a multi -year grid and 
electrification investment cycle. Power availability and grid 
capacity are emerging bottlenecks to the AI infra buildout. 
Addressing these constraints requires capex and time, which 
supports strong earnings visibility for grid equipment makers. 
Japan Banks : BoJ policy normali sation drives net interest 
margin upside, while Japan’s reflation cycle supports nominal 
loan demand, corporate capex and wealth activity. 
Governance reform is a re-rating driver  independent of the 
rate cycle. This includes recycling legacy holdings towards 
growth, buybacks and dividends.  
MSCI Taiwan: Taiwan’s foundry leadership makes it a critical 
supplier to global tech firms, while its broadening AI hardware 
supply chain is driving strong revenue growth.  
Global High Dividend  payers: Provides a higher -income 
equity allocation with historically lower volatility than the MSCI 
ACWI and modest downside cushioning on balance.  
US Communication Services: We remain constructive and 
expect further upward earnings revisions, driven by improving 
digital ad-spending and AI monetisation. 
Sector views: Continue to broaden & diversify 
We upgrade US Materials to Overweight as robust capex 
drives demand for industrial metals and construction 
materials, while we remain bullish on gold . US Technology 
and Communication Services remain the primary growth 
engines, supported by structural AI tailwinds. We remain 
Overweight in Financials across the US, Europe ex -UK, and 
Japan, underpinned by earnings stability  and elevated rates 
supporting interest income. This is further supported by a US 
capital markets recovery  and European balance -sheet 
strength. We remain Overweight in Europe ex-UK Industrials, 
reflecting EU fiscal stimulus and Germany's infrastructure 
acceleration legislation. 
In China, we broaden our Overweight exposure by upgrading 
three sectors: Materials (benefiting from structurally elevated 
commodity prices), Healthcare (biotech innovation and a robust 
pipeline sustaining growth), and Financials (easing margin 
pressure, fee recovery, and capital market reform). Our existing 
Overweight positio ns in Technology and Communication 
Services maintain exposure to China's AI build-out, with fiscal 
deployment into high-tech infrastructure serving as a near-term 
catalyst. On the other hand, we downgrade Utilities to 
Underweight on capped margins and retain our Underweight 
views on Real Estate and Consumer Staples.

Gold outlook:  Gold has remained resilient despite a more 
hawkish Fed and higher real yields, suggesting much of the 
tightening is already reflected in prices. With markets 
discounting an aggressive Fed path, we see an increasingly 
asymmetric risk -reward: limited downsid e risk from further 
rate repricing, but meaningful upside should Fed rate 
expectations shift towards being less restrictive. Official -
sector demand has slowed from recent years but has become 
broader based, while investor positioning remains well below 
levels seen at the start of the 2022 hiking cycle. 
Oil outlook: Oil prices have risen sharply as recent attacks 
cast greater uncertainty over Saudi export s, even as Gulf 
crude flows overall remain resilient. Other regional producers 
have helped sustain exports, containing the immediate supply 
impact. However, adequate US commercial crude stocks 
mask thinner buffers elsewhere, with strategic reserves and 
fuel inventories low. This leaves the m arket more vulnerable 
to a prolonged disruption, supporting a higher geopolitical risk 
premium and an upward revision to our outlook.

15 Gold, crude oil – at a glance

Anthony Naab, CFA 
Investment Strategist

### Page 19

Global Market Outlook 19

USD view

Further Fed tightening supports the USD near term. We expect the USD to remain supported, with our 3-month DXY forecast 
at 100.2. Resilient US payrolls for August alongside sticky energy and IT price -driven inflation reinforce the Fed's focus on 
containing inflation. Following its 25bp hike to 3.75–4.00%, we expect another hike by end-2026 and potentially another one in 
early H1 2027. Further tightening should keep US front -end yields elevated and support the USD. However, money markets 
already price a more aggressive Fed hiking path than our expectations, limiting the scope for further USD gains and leaving the 
USD vulnerable if incoming data weaken the case for additional hikes. 
Fading inflation and narrowing rate advantage point to a softer USD in the medium term. We expect the USD index (DXY) 
to decline gradually towards 98 over 12 months. Inflation pressures are expected to moderate  as the impact of oil prices and 
tariffs fade, reducing the need for continued Fed tightening. We forecast that the Fed funds rate may peak in H1 2027  in the 
current mini-rate hike cycle and then gradually decline thereafter as inflation moves closer towards target , supporting our DXY 
call. Further ECB and BoJ tightening should also progressively narrow the USD's relative rate advantage, supporting gradual 
USD moderation. Resilient US growth and elevated government bond yields should, however, limit the pace of depreciation.

Near-term EUR weakness; constructive medium term. The ECB raised its deposit 
rate 25bps to 2.50% in September. Potential ECB tightening should narrow the US-
Euro area rate differential over time, supporting EUR over 12 months.

GBP/USD 1.3219 1.33 1.35 
Rangebound with near term downside risk: UK inflation rose to 3.1% y/y, keeping 
BoE tightening risk alive, but softer labour conditions should limit the extent of 
tightening. Fed -BoE policy divergence may constrain GBP near term, while an 
expected peak in US rates by mid -2027 should allow a modes t recovery over the 
medium term. The Autum Budget in October could increase volatility.

USD/JPY 158.86 157 153 
Constructive JPY. The BoJ's September 25bp hike to 1.25% reinforces policy 
normalisation. The still wide  US-Japan yield differential may limit near-term JPY 
gains, but further BoJ tightening should gradually narrow the gap and support JPY 
over the medium term towards our target of 153.

AUD/USD 0.7012 0.73 0.74 
Near-term AUD weakness; constructive medium term: Australia’s Q2 GDP grew 
0.4% q/q, reinforcing domestic resilience. A relatively restrictive RBA stance supports 
AUD, while eventual narrowing in relative rate differentials should improve the 
medium-term outlook.

NZD/USD 0.5662 0.56 0.57 
Rangebound: The RBNZ raised rates 25bps to 2.75%, while New Zealand’s Q2 
GDP grew 0.2% q/q. Improving growth provides support, but an uneven recovery and 
near-term USD strength should limit NZD gains.

USD/CAD 1.4138 1.39 1.37 
Near-term CAD weakness; modest medium-term recovery: Canada employment 
fell 41,700 in August vs. a 15,000 expected rise, while the BoC held rates at 2.25%. 
Softer labour conditions and Fed-BoC divergence may constrain CAD near term.

USD/CHF 0.8278 0.82 0.79 
Near-term CHF weakness; medium-term CHF recovery: Swiss inflation surprised 
higher at 0.8% y/y, but the wide Fed -SNB rate differential should continue to favour 
USD near term. As Fed tightening peaks and the US rate advantage narrows, CHF 
should gradually recover over 12 months.

USD/CNH 6.7159 6.70 6.67 
Gradual CNH appreciation: China’s industrial output in August rose 5.2% y/y, 
beating estimates, but retail sales growth slowed to 0.4% on weak domestic demand. 
Production resilience and PBoC currency support favour modest CNH appreciation, 
while soft consumption and an easing policy bias should limit the pace of gains.

USD/SGD 1.2794 1.28 1.26 
Rangebound near term; constructive SGD medium term: Further Fed tightening 
and elevated US yields may limit SGD appreciation near term. Resilient 
fundamentals and MAS's exchange -rate policy should support gradual SGD 
appreciation as US rate support fades.

USD/INR 95.96 – 98 
Modestly bearish INR: India’s crude oil imports rose 26% y/y in August, and a USD 
77bn AI import deficit are both adding to structural import pressures. Higher -for-
longer US rates remain a headwind, while capital inflows and RBI intervention should 
smooth out near-term volatility and support a gradual INR depreciation towards 98.

USD/MYR 4.0865 – 4.00 Modestly bullish MYR: Resilient domestic demand, strong exports and corporate FX conversion support MYR; external uncertainty should keep appreciation gradual.

USD/KRW 1,368.9 – 1,540 
Bearish KRW: Recent KRW -supportive corporate USD selling is fading, while 
reduced National Pension Service’s FX hedging and prospect of USD 350bn US 
investment package by the South Korean national investment committee could 
increase USD demand. Higher US rates add a he adwind, supporting KRW 
depreciation over 12 months.

### Page 21

Global Market Outlook 21

Summary

Our stock-bond model (3-6 months) reduced its Overweight allocation to global equities to +1 from +3 (maximum +5) 
in September. The downgrade was driven primarily by a weaker valuation signal as valuation score fell to -2 for both Developed 
Market and Asian equities. Despite some moderation, fundamentals remain strongly supportive of equities. Positive factors 
include resilient net earnings upgrades, a positive economic surprise index, and PMI new orders remaining above 50, signalling 
continued expansion in industrial activity. Market technicals weakened but continued to support equities overall. Our market 
breadth indicator shows that 72% of equity markets remain above their 200 -day moving averages. Stock net advances are 
positive and not at levels that would typically signal an imminent market reversal. The stock-bond model continues to outperform 
strongly, generating 4.2% alpha YTD relative to the 60/40 equity-bond benchmark. 
Our 1–3 month equity market regime models continue to signal low bear market risk, with probabilities of just 0.7% for 
S&P 500 and 5.0% for MSCI AC World  indices. The low readings continued to be driven by subdued option -market implied 
volatility. At 14 – 15%, the VIX and market cap -weighted one-month implied volatility measures for Europe and US remain 
comfortably below the 20% threshold at which bear market risk tends to rise sharply. While momentum indicators have softened, 
they point to a typical market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful market stress.  
Our positioning indicators are not signalling any reversal risk. Previous long positioning in DXY has fully unwound and our 
market diversity indicator for MSCI Singapore has also normalized after the index corrected from its September peak.

15 Quant perspective: Reducing Equity 
Overweight as Valuation Weakened

Francis Lim Senior Quantitative Strategist Maggie, Au Yeung Quantitative Analyst

### Page 22

Global Market Outlook 22 
Fig. 36 Long- and short-term quantitative models remain bullish on risk assets 
Long-term models below have a typical time horizon of 3-6 months, while short-term models have a 1-3-month horizon

Long-term Stock or bond US recession model 
Current view Reduce equity Overweight 20% probability within the next 6 months 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Fundamental: +2. 3-month change in PMI new 
orders indicate a slower pace in the expansion of 
industrial activity. All other factors such as net 
earnings upgrades, economic surprise index and 
risk sentiment remain positive.  
• Valuation: -2. Both DM and Asian equities are 
expensive as their valuation scores exceeded 1 
standard deviation of historical average.  
• Technical: +1. Net-advances in global stocks are 
positive but not extreme. Market breadth declined 
as equity markets above their 200-day moving 
averages fell from 85% to 72%, but it remains 
above the model’s 60% threshold. 
Estimated probability hovers around 20 – 21% 
• Conference Board consumer confidence 
remains weak at -8.6% y/y. 
• PMI new orders-to-inventory ratio is at 1.1x, 
below its historical average of 1.2x.  
• Conference Board leading indicators 
contracted by -1.1% y/y.  
• Fed funds rate-to-natural rate ratio is still high 
at 1.6x, reflecting a restrictive monetary policy 
compared to historical average of 1.3x. 
Key model 
factors 
• Economic activity, macro risk and surprise indices, 
corporate earnings, forward P/E ratio and technical 
factors. 
• Conference Board leading indicator, Consumer 
Confidence Index, ISM New Orders and Fed 
funds rate. 
How does it 
work? 
• A monthly scorecard of -5 to 5 based on 
fundamentals, valuations and market breadth 
factors to indicate the relative preference for bonds 
and equities. A positive score favours equities and 
vice versa. 
• A probability model based on regression. Long-
term statistical tests are conducted to identify 
the four key economic factors above.

Short-term Technical analysis Investor positioning 
Current views Bullish S&P 500 & MSCI AC World indices None flagged 
What factors 
is this view 
based on? 
• Bullish S&P500 and MSCI AC World. The 
probabilities of bear market sit at just 0.7% and 
5.0%, respectively, driven by subdued options-
market implied volatility. Momentum across both 
indices have weakened but they point to a typical 
market consolidation rather than signs of meaningful  
market stress.  
• Previous long positioning in the USD index 
(DXY) has fully unwound after DXY fell 
steeply form its peak in late July. The renewed 
strength in DXY since the Fed’s 16 Sept rate 
hike has started from neutral positioning.  
• Market diversity indicator for MSCI 
Singapore has normalised after the index 
corrected from its September peak. 
Key model 
factors 
• Market factors: Momentum, volatility, interest rate 
differentials, relative returns, inflation swap rates, 
economic surprises, etc. 
• Price action: Overbought conditions occur 
when prices rise sharply; oversold conditions 
happen when prices fall rapidly in a short time.  
How does it 
work? 
• Scanning through 7,000+ factors, the framework 
uses Machine Learning to forecast market regimes 
or future trends based on identified market drivers. 
• A market indicator based on fractal analysis 
that provides a timely indication of investor 
positioning based on price actions.

Source: Standard Chartered

### Page 27

Global Market Outlook 27

Time is your most precious commodity – be sure to spend it wisely 
Time is undoubtedly valuable. The days may seem long, but the years are short. So, make the choice 
to spend your time wisely. Whether you’re setting out on your investment journey, navigating the 
intricacies of mid -life wealth planning or fortifying assets  for your golden years, invest time today to 
ensure your wealth strategy is aligned to what’s right for you – Today, Tomorrow and Forever. 
Setting aside the time now to review your plan will pay dividends in the future. Markets have moved. 
Your portfolio’s current asset allocation may no longer be optimally positioned to maximise the 
opportunities ahead. Ask yourself the following: Am I holding too much cash? Am I sufficiently allocating 
to growth assets for the long term? Is my portfolio diversified? Am I capturing the best opportunities? 
And most importantly, is my wealth working hard for me so that I don’t have to? 
Use our SC Wealth Select framework and advisory specialists to help guide you through this process.

Purpose 
Today,  
Tomorrow,  
Forever 
Our approach to helping you grow and manage 
your wealth starts with you. We use a goals -
aware approach to understand your vision of 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever for yourself, your 
family and beyond, and then design portfolios to 
meet your various needs.  
Using our ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ 
approach, we ensure your wealth needs for the 
near term (Today) are met, while ensuring your 
wealth needs for the decades ahead (Tomorrow 
and Forever) are also planned for.  
Your vision of ‘Today, Tomorrow and Forever’ is 
unique to you. Our specialists partner with you to 
build well -diversified, long -term Foundation 
portfolios, aligned to your Today, Tomorrow and 
Forever needs. Opportunistic ideas are added to 
capture short -term opportunities, a nd sufficient 
protection is included to address the objectives of 
you and your family. 
Today, Tomorrow and Forever 
Approach

Principles 
that stand the 
test of time 
Adhering to time-tested principles, to ensure your 
investment decisions remain robust and 
consistently applied, is paramount to your 
success Today, Tomorrow and Forever. We use 
five Wealth Principles to guide and guardrail your 
wealth decisions.

Discipline – ensure consistency and 
prudence over your emotions 
• Reacting to emotions, such as optimism and 
fear, can lead to poor investment decisions at 
the worst times 
• Have a plan and stick to it – this helps you to 
stay focused on the bigger picture

Diversification – simply put, don’t put 
all your eggs in one basket 
• Reduce risk by holding a variety of financial 
assets. Multi -asset diversification in your 
Foundation portfolio is important 
• As a guide, make sure your portfolio contains 
a variety of asset classes and investments 
that have low correlation with one another

Time in the Market – a more robust 
strategy than timing the market

• Predicting market selloffs is challenging, and 
timing your exit and re-entry is difficult 
• Missing out on the best performing days of a 
market can have a significantly detrimental 
impact on your portfolio 
• ‘Time in the market’ and buying the market 
with a longer -term view provide more 
consistent returns that can ride out bumps 
along the way

Risk and Return – make sure the risk 
is worth the return 
• To achieve higher investment returns, you will 
likely have to accept a greater level of risk in 
your portfolio 
• Therefore, it’s important to understand the 
risks and manage these on an ongoing basis

Protection – don’t let the unexpected 
catch you unprepared 
• Even though you may feel healthy, or 
financially stable now, protection offers the 
ability to overcome times of financial 
uncertainty and mitigate the long-term impact 
of unforeseen events on your wealth 
• A good protection plan not only safeguards 
your wealth today, but also considers the 
value of your future earnings over your 
lifetime, in today’s terms

## Document 350: Postcard from Bangkok IMF meetings_ don’t take resilience for granted (2026-10-19)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Postcard from Bangkok IMF meetings_ don’t take resilience for granted (2026-10-19).pdf
Report source: BNP Paribas
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

The bank
for a changing
world
3
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026EDITORIAL
POSTCARD FROM BANGKOK IMF MEETINGS: DON’T TAKE RESILIENCE FOR GRANTED
1 See EcoNews for more details.
The global economy’s unexpected resilience might have invited the self-congratulation that is a trademark of internatio-
nal official meetings. But policymakers gathered in Bangkok for the 2026 IMF Annual Meetings were wearily aware that
“continued resilience cannot be taken for granted”, as noted in the prologue to the closing Chair’s Statement to the Inter-

national Monetary and Financial Committee meeting. Growth forecasts remain in line with those made a year ago, when
interest rates were much lower and no energy shock was in the cards, and even with those made in April, when a short
shock was the base case. But what has sustained global growth against the odds so far could falter before the headwinds
ease: oil buffers are being depleted; fiscal policy urgently needs to shift from support to deficit reduction as interest rates

remain persistently higher; and the AI boom that has been a salvatory growth engine comes with serious questions about
its sustainability and long-term economic effects. In addition to these global issues, the combination of fiscal challenges
and elections in France and the US were seen as key risks, while some topics that had until recently been in the spotlight
found themselves overshadowed.
BETTER GROWTH THAN EXPECTED FOR MOST , WORSE INFLATION FOR ALL

Despite the energy shock and surge in interest rates, the IMF's
latest global growth forecast is unchanged from a year ago and April
— at 3.1% in 2026 and 3.4% in 2027 — although the geographical
breakdown has shifted: war-exposed countries and many energy im -
porters have been downgraded, while AI-exposed countries have been
upgraded. Asia is the standout winner, expected to account for 68% of
global growth in 2026 — a feat for a region highly dependent on Gulf

energy. Compared to July, the growth forecast for advanced economies
is revised up to 2.1% in 2026 (+0.4pp, driven by Germany) and remains
at 1.8% in 2027. Among emerging markets, the IMF upgraded Asia and
downgraded other regions. Meanwhile, global headline inflation is ex-
pected to rise to 4.7% in 2026, mainly due to energy and food, before
easing to 4.1% in 2027 (revised +0.2pp)1.
AI OPTIMISM DOMINATES, BUT WITH SOME HEAVY UNANSWERED
QUESTIONS

The topic of AI dominated the Meetings. The broad consensus was that
it is transformational and offers great opportunities to all countries.
But, for now, it is producing a mixed bag of economic effects: a power-
ful boost to global demand, directly through an investment and trade
boom and indirectly through wealth effects from stock-market gains;
but also much higher interest rates (fuelled by the borrowing needed
to fund the AI build-out, and higher prices for chips, all the products

they go into, and the electricity and water data centres need).
Beyond this shared diagnosis, strong arguments could be heard on both
sides of several critical questions. The most pressing being: will AI ge-
nerate earnings commensurate with current expectations? If not — or
even if expectations are merely downgraded — expect large declines in
AI-related asset prices, a sudden stop in funding and a significant blow
to growth wherever AI has been a key engine. This is not in anyone’s

baseline for next year, but it is clearly a top risk. To what extent will AI
boost productivity over five or ten years? The IMF incorporates almost
no boost in its five-year baseline, but presents an upside scenario in
which it does. Will AI create more jobs than it destroys, raise or reduce
labour’s share of income, prove inflationary or deflationary beyond the
near term, and strengthen cyber resilience or turbocharge attacks?
And how should it be taxed to preserve the fiscal base and offset unde-

sirable labour-market effects?
A MIXED POLICY SCORECARD
Monetary policy has appropriately taken a hawkish turn nearly eve -
rywhere, and an increase in rate rises is likely in key economies, though
not enough to threaten growth. Eurozone central bankers were parti -
cularly clear in sticking to their “moderate response” playbook.
Fiscal policy, however, has yet to adjust to the end of cheap debt. With
public debt high in most countries, a growing share of national bud -

gets will be absorbed by interest payments. Yet very few countries are
consolidating. The IMF’s top call to action was to address this urgent-
ly, especially among the worst offenders — the US and France. Fiscal
restraint would also help central banks curb inflation, reducing the
need for higher interest rates.
Policies to redress global imbalances are not closer to being adop -
ted than at previous IMF Meetings, but they were not particular -
ly highlighted by the IMF this time, beyond the mantra that “the US

needs to save more, China to consume more, and Europe to invest
more”. There were reportedly good discussions behind closed doors
— at the G7 on “non-market policies and practices (NMPPs)” — and
encouraging early steps in a long journey in Europe-China talks. That
is better than nothing, but hardly enough to expect progress within a
reasonable timeframe. Meanwhile, the risks of a disorderly unwind will
keep rising.
TWO ZONES OF CONCERN IN FOCUS: FRANCE AND THE US

France was a top concern for many participants. They feared a repeat
of the 2010–2012 Eurozone debt crisis, seeing a country with public
debt approaching 120% of GDP , sub-par growth, a poor record of fiscal
consolidation, riots in the streets and presidential elections six mon -
ths away, with populist candidates leading the polls. French and Eu -
ropean policymakers, alongside private-sector participants, presented
a different picture, namely: a fiscal situation that is serious, but is

being managed (the draft budget provides for meaningful adjustment
and is very likely to be adopted without being weakened); a widening
consensus from centre-left to far-right which favours a return to fiscal
responsibility, in line with a similar shift in public opinion; and the
electoral process is only beginning, with the outcome still wide open —
not least because the candidates’ names and numbers are not known.

### Page 2

4
economic-research.bnpparibas.comEcoWeek 26.38 / 19 October 2026
The bank
for a changing
world
It was also pointed out that the quantum of fiscal adjustment required
is not out of line with previous consolidation episodes in France in the
2010s and the 1990s. Still, most participants left not much reassured
and expecting a bumpy ride ahead.
US participants outside government saw a growing likelihood of a De -
mocratic sweep in the midterm elections, Either way, they expected

little fiscal consolidation, despite a manifestly challenging fiscal path
that may end up causing volatility in financial markets. Non-US parti -
cipants also feared growing policy unpredictability.
OVERSHADOWED TOPICS: THE STARS OF RECENT IMF MEETINGS
Tariffs were entirely absent from the agenda and conversations. Toke-
nised finance (rather than the narrower topic of stablecoins) still fea -
tured, but far less prominently: as the field takes shape, discussion

has shifted from the conceptual what and why to the technical how.
Climate change and low-income countries remained on the agenda,
but far less in focus than at previous Meetings. The sheer number of
issues demanding attention may be overwhelming financial policyma-
kers’ bandwidth.
*****
All in all, while the global context remains extraordinarily challenging,
it’s comforting to see that global financial policy makers are broadly
alert to both risks and opportunities, and to what needs to be done for

the latter to prevail. Now they need to deliver.
Isabelle Mateos y Lago
isabelle.mateosylago@bnpparibas.com

## Document 355: Rates Spark_ French spreads singed by violent protests

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rates Spark_ French spreads singed by violent protests.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-10-19
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

French spreads remain under pressure as protests reinforce
concerns that any future government will struggle to deliver
the reforms needed to stabilise public ﬁnances. At the same
time, the ECB’s hands are largely tied by energy prices still
pressuring inﬂation
Benjamin Schroeder
French government bond spreads continue to widen versus Germany on the
ﬁscal situation and more recently the protests
France remains one of the focal issues in the eurozone. The spread

of French government bonds over Germany has come under
renewed widening pressure against the backdrop of violent protests
grabbing the headlines.
The protests inject more uncertainty into the dynamics ahead of
the presidential elections. On the right, Le Pen has jumped on public
unrest to promote her law-and-order platform. Others have shifted
the blame to the left. And indeed, if betting markets are to be
trusted, the beneﬁciaries appear to be the right and centre

candidates, while on the far left, Mélenchon’s chances of winning

### Page 2

the presidential race have been pared back of late. For bonds, that
might be the only consolation, as his plans are viewed among the
most controversial.
More importantly, the protests highlight that any government will
struggle to implement the required reforms and savings against
broader public resistance.
Tuesday will likely see more headlines around the 2027 budget, as
Parliament is slated for a ﬁrst formal vote on the revenue section of
the ﬁnance bill. From a ﬂurry of proposed amendments, what

remains is tax revenues being pared back and tax breaks expanded.
It appears parties are still preoccupied with positioning themselves
for the upcoming elections rather than tackling the ﬁscal issue.
It is not the end of the debate, though. Following the revenue
section is the social security budget and then the expenditure part
of the ﬁnance bill. All bills will be passed on to the Senate and
eventually back to Parliament for a ﬁnal vote. That is, in theory. The

government still has the option to bypass Parliament to push
through its ﬁscal consolidation plans, though at the risk of a no-
conﬁdence vote.
For now, a lack of will to make unpleasant but needed choices is
likely to keep spreads over Bunds on a path towards testing recent
intraday highs, with the 10y spread at 158bp. On an end-of-day
basis, Monday’s close at 147bp already marked a new high since
the sovereign debt crisis. We have been looking for the spread to

remain in the 125–150bp range. But negative headlines are piling
up, and despite some dovish ECB remarks around the tightening of
ﬁnancial conditions, energy market pressures are tying the ECB’s
hands. A next test for French spreads awaits later this week with a
scheduled rating review from Moody’s.
Tuesday’s events and market view
Following the UK jobs data in the morning, the next focus in
European data is the German ZEW index, which is again
expected to show an improving outlook for businesses. The

### Page 2

UNITED STATES 
The US economy is expected to grow above its potential in 2026, with an average annual growth rate of 2. 2%, close to the 2025 rate (2.1%) . 
This apparent resilience to the uncertainty, tariff and energy shocks, together with above-trend productivity growth, masks K-shaped growth, 
driven by investment linked to AI-optimism and consumption by the wealthiest amid a wealth effect (historically high stock market valuations). 
Inflation overshooting is set to continue (3.5% in 2026) at least through 2028, largely because of the rise in oil prices and tariffs – although the 
impact of these latter appears to be less significant than expected. The labour market is showing clear signs of improvement that should bring 
the unemployment rate down toward 4.0%. Given this shift in price and employment risks amid dynamic growth, the FOMC proceeded with a 
first rate hike at September’s meeting and we expect two further 25bp increases in December 2026 and January 2027, bringing the Fed Funds 
target range at 4.25% - 4.50%.

CHINA 
Economic growth rebounded to 4.5% y/y in Q3 2026 (1.2% q/q) after 4.3% y/y in Q2 and 5.0% in Q1. It is projected to reach 4.6% in 2026 as a 
whole, down from 5% in 2025. Growth remains characterised by a K-shaped trajectory, and the gap between the strong performance of exports 
and the fragility of sectors that rely on domestic demand has widened this year. Retail sales barely increased in the first nine months of 2026, 
the crisis in the property sector has continued and household confidence remains low. Fiscal policy measures aimed at supporting investment 
and private consumption will increase over the last months of the year . CPI inflation has rebounded slightly since last March, mostly due to 
the rise in global energy prices, as well as due to the increase in electronic good prices and anti-involution measures implemented by the 
authorities. Deflationary pressures persist, however, given weakness in domestic demand.

EUROZONE 
Eurozone growth is expected to slow to 1.1% in 2026 (from 1.3% in 2025), before strengthening to 1.5% in 2027, supported by higher defence 
and infrastructure spending as well as the global AI capex cycle. The economy has proven resilient to the energy shock, notab ly thanks to the 
increasingly tangible impact of G erman defence spending on the real economy. Elevated energy prices in the autumn (especially for natural 
gas), combined with resilient growth, are set to accelerate inflation to a peak around the turn of the year. Annual average i nflation is thus 
expected to reach  3.1% in 2026 (from 2.1% in 2025), before easing to 3.0% in 2027. Given persistent inflation and resilient growth, we 
anticipate another 25bp hike from the ECB in December 2026, bringing the deposit rate to 2.75%, followed by an extended pause . That said, 
stronger than expected second-round effects could lead to more tightening than our current baseline.

### Page 3

FRANCE 
GDP growth remained low in Q2 (0% after -0.2% in Q1) as a result of deteriorated agricultural and construction output, as well as higher 
inflation (2.5% in 2026 according to our forecast, after 0.9% in 2025) . In 2026, GDP growth should  reach 0.5% (after 0.9% in 2025), driven 
mainly by exports.

UNITED KINGDOM 
UK GDP growth would improve slightly to 1.3% in 2026 and remain at this level in 2027  (1.2% in 2025). The economy has so far weathered 
both the energy price shock and domestic political volatility. That said, growth is expected to decelerate in the second half  of the year, while 
inflation is set to remain stubbornly above target (averag ing 3.2% in 2026 and 3.4% in 2027), driven by higher energy prices and mounting 
second-round effects. In response, we expect the BoE to deliver a single insurance hike in November 2026, bringing the Bank Rate to 4.00%. 
The BoE is then expected to resume easing in H2 2027, with two cuts taking the Bank Rate to 3.50% by end-year.

JAPAN 
Following 1.2% in 2025, Japan's GDP growth is expected to decelerate to 1.0% in 2026, before rebounding to 1.2% in 2027. Higher inflation 
and production costs associated with the energy shock are expected to weigh on the economy’s overall performance. On the other hand, the 
latter is supported by fiscal policy  and AI-related investment. Inflation has generally overshot the 2% y/y target since 2022. Accordingly, the 
Bank of Japan initiated a cautious process of “adjustment in the degree of monetary accommodation” in 2024, lifting the policy rate to 1.25% 
so far (previously negative) – the highest since 1995. We expect two more hikes by the end of Q1 2027, followed by a return to a more cautious 
pace until the terminal rate of +2.5% is reached in H2 2028. Japan is facing long-term rates pressure, illustrated by historically high 10- and 30-
year yields, probably fueled by the level of public debt and the pace of monetary adjustment.

EXCHANGE RATES 
We expect Fed hikes to temporarily offset structural headwinds and support the dollar. EUR/USD would target 1.14 by end -2026, before the 
dollar's structural downtrend resumes toward 1.20 by end-2027, although political and fiscal risk may weigh on the euro next year. The USD/JPY 
appreciation would continue but at a more moderate pace, with the BoJ still behind the curve despite its hikes. By contrast, GBP/USD would 
see relative support underpinned by a modest fiscal risk premium under the new Labour government, and resilient growth.

## Document 396: US Equity Views_ Over-earning, but not an earnings bubble

Source PDF: all report pdf/US Equity Views_ Over-earning, but not an earnings bubble.pdf
Report source: Goldman Sachs Publishing
Date: 2026-09-17
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 2

semiconductor share prices have recently lagged the trajectory of near-term EPS
estimates. Similarly, the market trades at a P/E discount relative to what a macro model
would imply given the current level of corporate profitability. Our 12-month S&P 500
return forecast of +14% (to 8,700) reflects the view that earnings growth, rather than
expanding valuations, will remain the primary driver of the bull market.
17 September 2026   2
Goldman Sachs US Equity Views

### Page 3

The magnitude of S&P 500 “over-earning”

S&P 500 earnings grew by an extraordinary 51% year/year in Q2 2026 and have 
grown by 26% during the past four quarters. During the last 30 years, the recent pace 
of earnings growth has been exceeded only during the post-recession rebounds in 2010 
and 2021.

The recent strength of S&P 500 earnings growth has raised investor concerns that 
current earnings power is unsustainable and that the market is in an “earnings 
bubble.” S&P 500 earnings have recently outpaced both their historical trend and their 
historical relationship with US GDP growth. S&P 500 4-quarter EPS growth has averaged 
7% during the past 30 years, and the relationship between S&P 500 earnings and US 
economic growth in recent decades would have pointed to a similar rate of earnings 
growth during the last few quarters.

Exhibit 1: S&P 500 trailing 4-quarter EPS has grown by 26% during the past year

### Page 4

The degree to which the US equity market is currently “expensive” depends in large 
part on the sustainability of recent earnings strength. The exceptional recent 
strength of earnings has driven an unusual wedge between equity valuation multiples 
based on near-term earnings and valuations on trend earnings. Because equity prices 
have failed to keep pace with surging earnings, near-term valuations show no hint of a 
bubble: The S&P 500 12-month P/E ratio has declined from 23x a year ago to 19x today 
and now matches its 10-year average. However, even an “average” multiple may be 
expensive if current earnings are unsustainable. The cyclically-adjusted P/E based on 
trailing 10-year earnings has climbed to one of its highest levels on record, falling shy of 
the highs of 1999-2000 but surpassing the level reached in 2021. Similarly, our 
colleagues have argued that the increase in market value of AI companies during the last 
few years requires an optimistic estimate of the future magnitude of AI-related profits 
and who will capture them.

### Page 5

The AI boom and S&P 500 earnings growth

Affirming investor concerns that US companies are “over-earning,” there are three 
factors that are boosting earnings today but will contribute to deceleration going 
forward: 1) AI capex spending, 2) semiconductor margin expansion, and 3) earnings 
from private investment gains.

AI capex 
The AI investment boom is driving nearly half of S&P 500 EPS growth this year, but 
its contribution will fade going forward. The mega-cap US hyperscalers are on track to 
spend $800 billion on capex this year, an increase of 94% vs. 2025. That spending is 
flowing through to the earnings of the AI infrastructure complex including 
semiconductor, tech hardware, industrials, and utilities companies, which are collectively 
accounting for roughly half of consensus S&P 500 earnings growth this year. The AI 
boom is also having a secondary impact on earnings outside of the infrastructure 
complex, for example by boosting capital markets activity and consumer wealth. Both 
consensus and GS analyst forecasts show hyperscaler capex growing at a slower rate in 
coming years, which should result in decelerating earnings growth for much of the AI 
infrastructure complex and a fading tailwind to S&P 500 earnings.

### Page 6

Hyperscaler depreciation expenses will continue to increase as capex growth 
decelerates, further dampening the boost of AI investment spending to S&P 500 
earnings growth. We estimate a drag from hyperscaler depreciation expenses on S&P 
500 earnings growth of 5pp in 2027, offsetting nearly half of the 11pp boost to earnings 
from capex spending. By 2028, the drag from depreciation should offset the S&P 500 
earnings uplift from continued capex spending.

### Page 7

If capex were fully expensed, consensus 2027 S&P 500 EPS would be 22% lower than 
under current accounting rules. The current gap between actual reported and 
hypothetical fully-expensed earnings is one of the widest in recent decades, exceeded 
only as earnings fell more quickly than capex during the recessions of 2001 and 2008.

Hyperscaler capex has consistently surprised relative to consensus estimates during 
the last few years, and the potential for additional surprises going forward creates a 
wide range of potential S&P 500 earnings outcomes. Our equity analysts expect 
hyperscaler capex will total $1.2 trillion in 2027 and $1.4 trillion in 2028, in which case 
the current tailwind from AI capex to S&P 500 EPS will fade by 2028. Exhibit 10 below 
outlines upside and downside scenarios around that base case. Next year, a $250 billion 
surprise in hyperscaler capex — in either direction — would shift S&P 500 EPS growth by 
about 6 pp in the same direction.  
We estimate that hyperscaler capex would need to decline by roughly 30%, to $570 
billion, to offset earnings growth for the rest of the S&P 500 in 2027. This decline 
would reduce earnings for the AI infrastructure complex by about 40%, leaving their 
profits about 25% above 2025 levels.

### Page 8

Semiconductor margin expansion 
A sharp increase in profit margins for many semiconductor companies has 
contributed to the above-trend recent pace of S&P 500 earnings growth. The 
combination of strong demand and limited supply has boosted semiconductor prices 
and gross margins, with the most pronounced margin expansion among memory stocks. 
S&P 500 memory firms currently enjoy gross margins of roughly 80%, more than double 
their historical average. Across the semiconductor industry, gross margins sit at their 
highest level in decades, and consensus expects margins to remain at similar levels 
through 2028.

Margin expansion has accounted for a large share of recent semiconductor earnings 
growth, but that boost should fade going forward. We estimate that about 1/4 of S&P 
500 Semiconductor earnings growth in 2026 is being driven by gross margin expansion. 
Our equity analysts believe the balance of memory supply and demand is likely to 
remain tight through 2027, with incremental supply additions unlikely to impact industry 
margins until 2028 at the earliest. However, they expect the rate of incremental margin 
expansion to slow meaningfully next year, reducing the contribution of semiconductors

S&P 500 earnings growth in the next few years will be highly sensitive to the

S&P 500 EPS growth by about 1 pp.

### Page 10

“Other income” from private investment gains 
Appreciating equity investment stakes are also temporarily inflating S&P 500 
earnings. Mega-cap tech earnings have recently been boosted by large GAAP “other 
income” related to their equity investments. In Q2 2026, the mega-cap tech companies 
enjoyed unrealized investment gains in private companies that totaled roughly $150 
billion, translating into 12% of S&P 500 EPS. We expect H2 2026 reports will include 
additional “other income” that should be larger than the gains in Q1 but smaller than Q2 
totals. We expect a much smaller contribution in 2027. The complete removal of this 
“other income” next year would create a drag of 8 pp on S&P 500 earnings growth in 
2027 relative to 2026, all else equal.

### Page 11

S&P 500 earnings outlook

Our baseline forecast is for S&P 500 EPS to rise to $415 in 2027 (+11% year/year) 
and to $460 in 2028 (+11%). We expect an above-consensus base of earnings in 2026 
due to further “other income” gains from private equity stakes in H2 of this year. 
Excluding the “other income” inflating earnings in 2026, S&P 500 earnings growth next 
year would register 18%. Our 2027 EPS forecast is similar to the bottom-up consensus 
estimate of $419 but above the median top-down strategist estimate of $403.

### Page 12

Our 2028 EPS forecast is about 5% below the bottom-up consensus, signaling a 
return to the typical historical pattern of downward revisions to consensus earnings 
estimates after the last two years of rising estimates. Outside of the complex of AI 
capex beneficiaries, we expect sales growth will register close to nominal GDP , margins 
will expand modestly, and a growing boost from AI productivity will result in earnings 
growth of 9% in both 2027 and 2028.  Our forecasts for EPS growth among this group of 
companies are roughly 4-5% below consensus estimates in both 2027 and 2028, similar 
to the historical average magnitude of downward revision to consensus estimates. While 
AI investment spending has had a direct impact on the earnings of semiconductor and 
other AI infrastructure stocks, it has also lifted earnings for companies outside of the “ AI 
complex,” including by boosting capital markets activity as well as supporting consumer 
spending via the wealth effect. 
Solid GDP growth and easing pressure from energy prices will be the main macro 
tailwinds for earnings growth in 2027. Economic growth is the primary variable in our 
top-down earnings model and explains more than half of the historical variation in S&P 
500 EPS growth. Our economists expect US real GDP growth will average 2.1% in 2027 
and 2.3% in 2028. Our commodity strategists expect Brent crude oil will decline to 
$80/bbl by the end of 2027, which would weigh modestly on Energy sector profits but 
boost earnings for most other sectors. Outside of the AI-related risks outlined above, 
persistent input cost pressures and risks to the consumer from equity market volatility 
are two of the key macro downside risks to our earnings forecast.

### Page 13

As the earnings tailwind from AI investment spending fades, the trajectory of S&P 
500 profitability in coming years will depend increasingly on the realization of AI 
productivity gains. Our forecasts embed a 1 pp boost to S&P 500 EPS growth from AI 
productivity in 2027 and a 2 pp boost in 2028. Economy-wide surveys and corporate 
commentary signal enterprise adoption that is increasing but remains in early stages, 
and the impact of AI adoption on corporate earnings still appears narrow. However, the 
recent acceleration in enterprise AI spending suggests that the earnings impact of AI 
adoption should become clearer in coming quarters.

### Page 14

Valuing S&P 500 earnings

Like our forecasts and consensus estimates, market pricing suggests investors 
anticipate a deceleration in earnings growth going forward. This is true both for the 
broad market and for AI infrastructure stocks. For example, the share prices of 
semiconductor stocks have historically mirrored the path of consensus EPS estimates, 
and the recent moderation of semiconductor returns has aligned with consensus 
estimates for decelerating growth in 2027 and 2028.

The current S&P 500 P/E multiple signals an optimistic outlook but market 
skepticism regarding the sustainability of current profits. In addition to rising interest 
rates, an anticipated deceleration in earnings helps explain the recent decline in the S&P 
500 forward P/E multiple. Along with macro factors like interest rates, the level of 
corporate profitability has helped explain the variation in S&P 500 valuation over time. 
Based on the historical relationship between S&P 500 ROE and P/E, the current S&P 500 
ROE of 24% — a record high — would point to a forward 12-month P/E of over 21x 
against today’s macro backdrop. Given current inflation and interest rates, today’s S&P 
500 multiple of 19x would be consistent with an ROE of roughly 22%, more than 200 bp 
below the current level but otherwise matching 2021 as the highest on record.

### Page 15

Earnings, rather than valuations, should continue to drive equity market upside 
from here. Our 3-, 6-, and 12-month S&P 500 return targets are +5 (8000), +9% (8300), 
and +14% (8700). These imply a P/E multiple on consensus forward EPS that remains 
close to the current level of 19x. Our economists and interest rate strategists expect 
interest rates to decline modestly in coming quarters, creating some macro upside risk to 
equity valuations. However, we expect investor uncertainty regarding the impact of AI 
on the outlook for long-term profits to persist in coming quarters, making a dramatic 
increase in valuations unlikely.

## Document 411: “换锚”不等于“降息”

Source PDF: all report pdf/“换锚”不等于“降息”.pdf
Report source: WallstreetCN (Global)
Date: 2026-10-20
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

⼴发宏观钟林楠认为，创设⾮银流动性⽀持⼯具直达公募基⾦和理财、政策利率锚定隔夜
品种、贷款定价基准⾛向多元、正式退出存贷款基准利率。这绝⾮简单 “ 类降息 ” ，⽽是货币
政策框架从数量型向价格型转型的关键⼀跃。债券市场勿形成单边预期，权益资产则应告
别单线索、拓宽配置⼴谱性。
摘要：
摘要
10 ⽉ 19 ⽇，央⾏在⾦融街论坛上推出若⼲项政策，其中有四项值得重点关注：（ 1 ）会同⾦融监
管总局、中国证监会设⽴应对⾮银机构⼤额赎回的流动性⽀持⼯具；（ 2 ）研究择机把公开市场
主要操作品种的期限切换为隔夜，公开市场隔夜回购利率将相应成为主要政策利率；（ 3 ）推动
贷款利率定价基准改⾰，从⽐较单⼀地参考贷款市场报价利率（ LPR ）定价向更多元的定价基准
转型；（ 4 ）退出存贷款基准利率。对这四项举措，我们有以下理解。
第⼀，⾸先是创设⾮银流动性⽀持⼯具，我们可以从流动性⻛险管理和宏观审慎框架优化的⻆
度去理解。官⽅的公开表述最早出现在去年 10 ⽉⾦融街论坛；今年 1 ⽉央⾏⼯作会议再次指出

“ 建⽴在特定情景下向⾮银机构提供流动性的机制性安排 ” ，且将其置于 “ 化解重点领域⾦融⻛险 ”
部分，显⽰这⼀⼯具设⽴初衷是防⻛险维护⾦融稳定。 6 ⽉央⾏在陆家嘴论坛上将措辞进⼀步升
级为 “ 研究设⽴特定情景⾮银流动性⽀持宏观审慎⼯具 ” ，将其进⼀步定义为宏观审慎⼯具⽽⾮货
币政策⽇常⼯具。⾦融街论坛上关于 “ 设⽴应对⾮银机构⼤额赎回的流动性⽀持⼯具 ” 的界定，⼀
是从 “ 研究设⽴ ” 到 “ 设⽴ ” ，显⽰政策进⼊落地阶段；⼆是进⼀步将 “ 特定情景 ” 具体为 “ ⾮银机构⼤
额赎回 ” ，明确了使⽤条件。
第⼆，⾮银流动性⽀持⼯具的要素和操作细节仍有待明确，⽬前有两点相对确定：（ 1 ）主要针
对债券市场。潘功胜⾏⻓指出 “ ⽬的是建⽴债券市场稳定机制，在资管机构⾯临群体性流动性⻛
险、债券市场出现赎回 — 抛售负向循环的特定情景下启⽤ ” 。近些年流动性⻛险和负反馈的典型
案例是 2022 年四季度，政策变化带来⻛偏和基本⾯预期上修，债券市场出现了 “ 利率快速上⾏ —

理财净值回撤 — 客⼾赎回 — 抛售债券 — 利率加速上⾏ — 理财净值进⼀步回撤 ” 的循环。在类似情
景下，央⾏只能通过加⼤对银⾏体系的流动性⽀持，借助 “ 银⾏ — ⾮银 ” 的流动性传递路径来稳定
⾮银负债进⽽稳定市场，政策效果会打折扣，在部分情景下（⽐如信⽤⻛险引发的负反馈）还
可能出现造成流动性淤积过于宽松，滋⽣资⾦空转套利；⽽设⽴新⼯具后，流动性可直达⾮银
机构，增强政策效⼒同时也避免流动性⼤幅波动；（ 2 ）操作可能是以 “ 互换 ” 形式，主要⾯向公募
基⾦、理财及其对应的证券⺟公司与银⾏⺟公司。依据《中国⼈⺠银⾏法》，央⾏不能直接对⾮
银机构发放贷款。今年 6 ⽉央⾏在陆家嘴论坛上提出研究 “ 机构群体性⾯临流动性危机且可能引发
系统性⻛险时，通过互换⽅式向⾮银机构提供紧急流动性 ” 。
第三，再来看公开市场操作主要品种切换为隔夜品种、政策利率切换为隔夜利率。7 ⽉中旬央⾏
曾提出逐步增加隔夜逆回购操作频次，之后隔夜逆回购操作频次和规模明显升⾼，市场对于进
⼀步改⾰有所预期。 10 ⽉以来 10 年和 30 年国债利率有不同程度的下⾏，其中可能包含对这⼀改

⾰趋势的定价。我们理解，这⼀举措主要⽬的是提⾼市场利率和政策利率期限的匹配度，完善
短端利率调控机制。今年年初以来，央⾏已多次向外传递市场基准利率应该更加关注 DR001 这
种隔夜品种，其成交量占⽐常年在 90% 以上，更能反映货币市场的真实情况，对于政策⽽⾔更
短期的市场基准利率调控难度会更低，海外⼤部分成熟央⾏都锚定隔夜品种；⽽央⾏主要操作
品种为 7 天逆回购， DR001 对标的政策利率为 7 天逆回购利率，在期限上明显错配。将公开市场
操作品种切换为隔夜、政策利率切换为隔夜逆回购利率，⼀则有助于提⾼流动性管理的精细
⾸⻚ 资讯 快讯 ⾏情 ⽇历 APP VIP 会员 ⼤师课 ⽣活家 搜索

### Page 2

度，更好的 “ 削峰填⾕ ” ，提⾼政策对市场利率的掌控⼒；⼆则实现市场利率与政策利率期限匹
配，理顺政策利率传导机制。
第四，关于贷款基准利率从单⼀转为多元基准，央⾏在今年⼀季度货币政策专栏中有过国际经
验的相关介绍；今年三季度部分银⾏已有相关实践，发放了利率挂钩 DR001 或 DR007 的贷款。
在前期报告《 26Q1 货政报告的六点增量信息》中，我们对其有过详细解读：主要经济体贷款利
率定价基准多元，⼀个重要原因在于直接融资的发展让企业部⻔能够以更低的成本、更加市场
化的利率来融资，进⽽倒逼贷款利率市场化。海外不少国家案例显⽰，融资渠道多元的⼤型企
业的贷款定价基准均是市场利率，⽽较难发债融资的中⼩型企业与零售客⼾贷款定价基准仍挂
钩政策利率。⽬前我国企业债券融资利率与贷款利率的利差已经明显拉开，直接融资对信贷的
替代作⽤愈发明显；为适应债券利率的下⾏，越来越多的贷款以 LPR 减点的利率来发放，今年上
半年 LPR 减点发放的贷款⽐重接近 50% ，明显⾼于 2019 年 LPR 改⾰时的⽔平。为适应直接融资

的发展、保持 LPR 的引导作⽤、促进社会综合融资成本低位运⾏，我国贷款有必要逐步转向多元
基准利率定价。在 LPR 之外，银⾏若再有意愿选择某⼀市场利率作为补充性贷款基准利率，理论
上⾄少需满⾜两个条件：⼀是能充分反映银⾏负债成本，⼆是波动偏低。由此推断，未来政策
稳定货币市场利率、降低波动的必要性会更为凸显。从 6 ⽉陆家嘴论坛⾄此次⾦融街论坛，央⾏
创设隔夜逆回购品种、将隔夜逆回购作为主要操作品种、政策利率切换为隔夜利率、缩窄隐性
利率⾛廊（临时隔夜正逆回购利率区间）等完善短端利率调控举措，与贷款基准利率多元化相
辅相成。
第五，退出存贷款基准利率更多是完成制度上的收尾、补全利率市场化框架的完整性，如央⾏
所指出的 “ ⾦融机构存贷款基准利率⾃ 2015 年 10 ⽉以来，⻓期未做调整，在存贷款定价中实际上
已不再发挥作⽤，⽬前只剩下在少数司法领域作为计算利息的依据 ” 。在⾦融领域，贷款定价⼤
致经历了三个阶段：（ 1 ） 2013 年之前盯住贷款基准利率；（ 2 ） 2013 年放开贷款利率管制，新定

价基准未形成，处于换锚的过渡期，实际定价中部分银⾏的贷款利率依然会参考贷款基准利
率；（ 3 ） 2019 年 LPR 改⾰之后，贷款利率定价锚定 LPR 。后续按照央⾏政策演进脉络，贷款基
准可能进⼀步变为 “LPR+ 市场利率（如 DR ） ” 。存款利率定价变化更加缓慢。央⾏在 2015 年名义
上放开存款利率管制，存款利率定价实际受⾃律机制牵引， 2022 年之后由于贷款利率单边跟随
LPR 和政策利率调整明显下⾏，存款利率定价调整缓慢，息差压⼒升⾼，央⾏宣布建⽴存款利率
调整机制，存款利率跟随 LPR 和 10 年国债利率调整，⽽ LPR 实际受政策利率（ 7 天逆回购利率）
牵引，所以在 2022 年之后，对于⾦融市场⽽⾔存贷款利率定价实际都统归于政策利率，利率传
导机制为 “ 政策利率 —LPR/10 年国债到期收益率 / 货币市场短端利率 — 存贷款利率与其他债券利
率 ” 。
第六，综上所述，我们不能简单地从 “ 类降息 ” 等狭义视⻆去理解政策举措，四项举措⼀⽅⾯有
助于维护流动性合理充裕、畅通利率传导；另⼀⽅⾯，它是既定的也是货币政策框架由数量型

调控向价格型调控转型、利率市场化改⾰的延续。关于政策利率与⼴谱利率以及实体融资成本
的后续传导机制和主要特征，仍具有⼀定复杂性，需要进⼀步观察。
第七，最后再看对资产定价的影响。对于债券市场来说，政策操作品种与利率切换、贷款基准
多元化等有助于降低央⾏投放资⾦的负债成本、稳定流动性，同时⼀则前期利率曲线已有初步
定价；⼆则同⽇出来的三季度 GDP 增速略超预期，利率所⾯临的经济基本⾯预期亦会有所上
修；三则从 9 ⽉单⽉固投和消费数据反弹来看，前期⼀揽⼦政策的效果也初步显现，所以要避免
形成对利率定价的单边预期。对于权益资产⽽⾔，前期主要定价全球流动性收敛、逆全球化、 AI
产业链供需关系等，对于国内政策和基本⾯的定价相对不⾜，⾦融街论坛期间推出的⼏项举措
有利于稳定增⻓条件，顺周期和内需相关资产将得到⼀定⽀撑，所以应告别单线索、适度提升
配置⼴谱性。
正⽂

### Page 3

10 ⽉ 19 ⽇，央⾏在⾦融街论坛上推出若⼲项政策，其中有四项值得重点关注：（ 1 ）会同⾦融监
管总局、中国证监会设⽴应对⾮银机构⼤额赎回的流动性⽀持⼯具；（ 2 ）研究择机把公开市场
主要操作品种的期限切换为隔夜，公开市场隔夜回购利率将相应成为主要政策利率；（ 3 ）推动
贷款利率定价基准改⾰，从⽐较单⼀地参考贷款市场报价利率（ LPR ）定价向更多元的定价基准
转型；（ 4 ）退出存贷款基准利率。对这四项举措，我们有以下理解。
⾸先是创设⾮银流动性⽀持⼯具，我们可以从流动性⻛险管理和宏观审慎框架优化的⻆度去理
解。从央⾏官⽹来看，官⽅的公开表述最早出现在去年 10 ⽉⾦融街论坛；今年 1 ⽉央⾏⼯作会议
再次指出 “ 建⽴在特定情景下向⾮银机构提供流动性的机制性安排 ” ，且将其置于 “ 化解重点领域
⾦融⻛险 ” 部分，显⽰这⼀⼯具设⽴初衷是防⻛险维护⾦融稳定。6 ⽉央⾏在陆家嘴论坛上将措
辞进⼀步升级为 “ 研究设⽴特定情景⾮银流动性⽀持宏观审慎⼯具 ” ，将其进⼀步定义为宏观审慎

⼯具⽽⾮货币政策⽇常⼯具。⾦融街论坛上关于 “ 设⽴应对⾮银机构⼤额赎回的流动性⽀持⼯具 ”
的界定，⼀是从 “ 研究设⽴ ” 到 “ 设⽴ ” ，显⽰政策进⼊落地阶段；⼆是进⼀步将 “ 特定情景 ” 具体为
“ ⾮银机构⼤额赎回 ” ，明确了使⽤条件。
2025 年 10 ⽉，央⾏在⾦融街论坛上指出，股票市场有⽀持资本市场稳定发展的两项货币政策⼯
具，⽀持汇⾦公司发挥 “ 类平准基⾦ ” 作⽤。综合平衡维护⾦融市场稳定运⾏和防范⾦融市场道德
⻛险，探索在特定情景下向⾮银机构提供流动性的机制性安排。
2026 年 1 ⽉，央⾏召开⼯作会议，会议指出，稳妥化解重点领域⾦融⻛险。建⽴在特定情景下向
⾮银机构提供流动性的机制性安排，发挥好两项⽀持资本市场的货币政策⼯具作⽤。
2026 年 6 ⽉，央⾏在陆家嘴论坛上指出，研究设⽴特定情景⾮银流动性⽀持宏观审慎⼯具。
2024 年 9 ⽉，⼈⺠银⾏会同证监会创设两项⽀持资本市场⼯具，建⽴了中央银⾏向⾮银机构和资
本市场的流动性供给渠道，发挥了积极的作⽤。在此基础上，我们继续研究政策⼯具，当债券

等市场出现系统性压⼒，正常流动性渠道受阻，机构群体性⾯临流动性危机且可能引发系统性
⻛险时，通过互换⽅式向⾮银机构提供紧急流动性。机制设计将综合权衡维护⾦融市场稳定运
⾏和防范⾦融市场道德⻛险。 “ 特定情景 ” 意味着该机制不是对⾮银⾦融机构的常态化流动性供
给； “ 防范⾦融市场道德⻛险 ” 意味着⾮银机构与央⾏操作必须满⾜宏观审慎要求，还要提供⾼等
级抵押品。
⾮银流动性⽀持⼯具的要素和操作细节仍有待明确，⽬前有两点相对确定：（ 1 ）主要针对债券
市场。潘功胜⾏⻓指出 “ ⽬的是建⽴债券市场稳定机制，在资管机构⾯临群体性流动性⻛险、债
券市场出现赎回 — 抛售负向循环的特定情景下启⽤ ” 。近些年流动性⻛险和负反馈的典型案例是
2022 年四季度，政策变化带来⻛偏和基本⾯预期上修，债券市场出现了 “ 利率快速上⾏ — 理财净
值回撤 — 客⼾赎回 — 抛售债券 — 利率加速上⾏ — 理财净值进⼀步回撤 ” 的循环。在类似情景下，
央⾏只能通过加⼤对银⾏体系的流动性⽀持，借助 “ 银⾏ — ⾮银 ” 的流动性传递路径来稳定⾮银负

债进⽽稳定市场，政策效果会打折扣，在部分情景下（⽐如信⽤⻛险引发的负反馈）还可能造
成流动性淤积过于宽松，滋⽣资⾦空转套利；⽽设⽴新⼯具后，流动性可直达⾮银机构，增强
政策效⼒同时也避免流动性⼤幅波动；（ 2 ）操作可能是以 “ 互换 ” 形式，主要⾯向公募基⾦、理财
及其对应的证券⺟公司与银⾏⺟公司。依据《中国⼈⺠银⾏法》，央⾏不能直接对⾮银机构发放
贷款。今年 6 ⽉央⾏在陆家嘴论坛上提出研究 “ 机构群体性⾯临流动性危机且可能引发系统性⻛险
时，通过互换⽅式向⾮银机构提供紧急流动性 ” 。
在此次⾦融街论坛上，央⾏指出，这项⼯具的⽬的是建⽴债券市场稳定机制，构成⼈⺠银⾏宏
观审慎管理的⼀项新⼯具。⼯具综合权衡防范⾦融市场体系的系统性⻛险和道德⻛险，在资管
机构⾯临群体性流动性⻛险、债券市场出现赎回 — 抛售负向循环的特定情景时启⽤；与央⾏开
展操作必须满⾜宏观审慎要求、提供⾼等级抵押品，防⽌⾦融市场定价扭曲、形成兜底幻觉，
维护市场纪律。⼯具适⽤的机构包括银⾏理财⼦公司、公募基⾦管理⼈，以及对应的银⾏⺟

⾏、证券⺟公司等。我们将在公开市场业务公告中明确⼯具操作相关要素。

### Page 4

再来看公开市场操作主要品种切换为隔夜品种、政策利率切换为隔夜利率。7 ⽉中旬央⾏曾提出
逐步增加隔夜逆回购操作频次，之后隔夜逆回购操作频次和规模明显升⾼，市场对于进⼀步改
⾰有所预期。 10 ⽉以来 10 年和 30 年国债利率有不同程度的下⾏，其中可能包含对这⼀改⾰趋势
的定价。我们理解，这⼀举措主要⽬的是提⾼市场利率和政策利率期限的匹配度，完善短端利
率调控机制。今年年初以来，央⾏已多次向外传递市场基准利率应该更加关注 DR001 这种隔夜
品种，其成交量占⽐常年在 90% 以上，更能反映货币市场的真实情况，对于政策⽽⾔更短期的
市场基准利率调控难度会更低，海外⼤部分成熟央⾏都锚定隔夜品种；⽽央⾏主要操作品种为 7
天逆回购， DR001 对标的政策利率为 7 天逆回购利率，在期限上明显错配。将公开市场操作品种
切换为隔夜、政策利率切换为隔夜逆回购利率，⼀则有助于提⾼流动性管理的精细度，更好的
“ 削峰填⾕ ” ，提⾼政策对市场利率的掌控⼒；⼆则实现市场利率与政策利率期限匹配，理顺政策

利率传导机制。
据新华⽹报道，今年 7 ⽉中旬，央⾏在国新办新闻发布会上指出，下⼀步，⼈⺠银⾏将继续平稳
有序推动货币政策操作框架的改⾰完善，更好引导市场隔夜利率在政策利率附近平稳运⾏。结
合⼀级交易商需求，研究逐步增加隔夜逆回购的操作频率，并和市场做好沟通。
关于贷款基准利率从单⼀转为多元基准，央⾏在今年⼀季度货币政策专栏中有过国际经验的相
关介绍；今年三季度部分银⾏已有相关实践，发放了利率挂钩 DR001 或 DR007 的贷款。在前期
报告《 26Q1 货政报告的六点增量信息》中，我们对其有过详细解读：主要经济体贷款利率定价
基准多元，⼀个重要原因在于直接融资的发展让企业部⻔能够以更低的成本、更加市场化的利
率来融资，进⽽倒逼贷款利率市场化。海外不少国家案例显⽰，融资渠道多元的⼤型企业的贷
款定价基准均是市场利率，⽽较难发债融资的中⼩型企业与零售客⼾贷款定价基准仍挂钩政策
利率。⽬前我国企业债券融资利率与贷款利率的利差已经明显拉开，直接融资对信贷的替代作
⽤愈发明显；为适应债券利率的下⾏，越来越多的贷款以 LPR 减点的利率来发放，今年上半年

LPR 减点发放的贷款⽐重接近 50% ，明显⾼于 2019 年 LPR 改⾰时的⽔平。为适应直接融资的发
展、保持 LPR 的引导作⽤、促进社会综合融资成本低位运⾏，我国贷款有必要逐步转向多元基准
利率定价。在 LPR 之外，银⾏若再有意愿选择某⼀市场利率作为补充性贷款基准利率，理论上⾄
少需满⾜两个条件：⼀是能充分反映银⾏负债成本，⼆是波动偏低。由此推断，未来政策稳定
货币市场利率、降低波动的必要性会更为凸显。从 6 ⽉陆家嘴论坛⾄此次⾦融街论坛，央⾏创设
隔夜逆回购品种、将隔夜逆回购作为主要操作品种、政策利率切换为隔夜利率、缩窄隐性利率
⾛廊（临时隔夜正逆回购利率区间）等完善短端利率调控举措，与贷款基准利率多元化相辅相
成。
据湖北⽇报报道，今年 7 ⽉招商银⾏向⼀家优质⺠营科技企业发放贷款 2000 万元，期限 1 年。该
笔贷款采⽤定价⽇前 3 个⽉ DR001 均值作为基准利率，并加点 87BP 确定执⾏利率，按 3 个⽉周期
进⾏重定价，实现贷款定价与货币市场利率挂钩，为 DR 基准利率在实体经济融资领域应⽤提供

实践案例。
退出存贷款基准利率更多是完成制度上的收尾、补全利率市场化框架的完整性，如央⾏所指出
的 “ ⾦融机构存贷款基准利率⾃ 2015 年 10 ⽉以来，⻓期未做调整，在存贷款定价中实际上已不再
发挥作⽤，⽬前只剩下在少数司法领域作为计算利息的依据 ” 。在⾦融领域，贷款定价⼤致经历
了三个阶段：（ 1 ） 2013 年之前盯住贷款基准利率；（ 2 ） 2013 年放开贷款利率管制，新定价基准
未形成，处于换锚的过渡期，实际定价中部分银⾏的贷款利率依然会参考贷款基准利率；（ 3 ）
2019 年 LPR 改⾰之后，贷款利率定价锚定 LPR 。后续按照央⾏政策演进脉络，贷款基准可能进
⼀步变为 “LPR+ 市场利率（如 DR ） ” 。存款利率定价变化更加缓慢。央⾏在 2015 年名义上放开存
款利率管制，存款利率定价实际受⾃律机制牵引， 2022 年之后由于贷款利率单边跟随 LPR 和政
策利率调整明显下⾏，存款利率定价调整缓慢，息差压⼒升⾼，央⾏宣布建⽴存款利率调整机
制，存款利率跟随 LPR 和 10 年国债利率调整，⽽ LPR 实际受政策利率（ 7 天逆回购利率）牵引，

所以在 2022 年之后，对于⾦融市场⽽⾔存贷款利率定价实际都统归于政策利率，利率传导机制
为 “ 政策利率 —LPR/10 年国债到期收益率 / 货币市场短端利率 — 存贷款利率与其他债券利率 ” 。

### Page 5

⻛险提⽰及免责条款
市场有⻛险，投资需谨慎。本⽂不构成个⼈投资建议，也未考虑到个别⽤⼾特殊的投资⽬标、财务状况或需要。⽤⼾应考虑本⽂
中的任何意⻅、观点或结论是否符合其特定状况。据此投资，责任⾃负。
217 收藏 分享到 :
写评论
图⽚
在此次⾦融街论坛上，央⾏指出，过去较⻓时间，央⾏直接规定⾦融机构的存贷款基准利率。
随着利率市场化改⾰推进，存贷款基准利率逐步被市场化的定价基准替代。银⾏贷款利率已经
有贷款市场报价利率、银⾏间隔夜回购利率等多个定价基准；⾃今年⼀季度开始，我们对外公
布了各个期限的存款平均利率，作为银⾏存款定价的参考。⾦融机构存贷款基准利率⾃ 2015 年
10 ⽉以来，⻓期未做调整，在存贷款定价中实际上已不再发挥作⽤，⽬前只剩下在少数司法领
域作为计算利息的依据。下⼀步，⼈⺠银⾏将推动这些领域的计息依据⽤市场化的利率指标来
替代，实现退出⾦融机构存贷款基准利率。
综上所述，我们不能简单地从 “ 类降息 ” 等狭义视⻆去理解政策举措，四项举措⼀⽅⾯有助于维护
流动性合理充裕、畅通利率传导；另⼀⽅⾯，它是既定的也是货币政策框架由数量型调控向价

格型调控转型、利率市场化改⾰的延续。关于政策利率与⼴谱利率以及实体融资成本的后续传
导机制和主要特征，仍具有⼀定复杂性，需要进⼀步观察。
10 ⽉以来，流动性明显变松， DR001 中枢降⾄ 1.3% 以下。对于这⼀轮宽松，我们在前期报告
《⾦融数据与流动性环境》中指出有⼀定的政策主导特征，主要⽬的是协同财政稳增⻓，稳定
市场。
最后再看对资产定价的影响。对于债券市场来说，政策操作品种与利率切换、贷款基准多元化
等有助于降低央⾏投放资⾦的负债成本、稳定流动性，同时⼀则前期利率曲线已有初步定价；
⼆则同⽇出来的三季度 GDP 增速略超预期，利率所⾯临的经济基本⾯预期亦会有所上修；三则
从 9 ⽉单⽉固投和消费数据反弹来看，前期⼀揽⼦政策的效果也初步显现，所以要避免形成对利
率定价的单边预期。对于权益资产⽽⾔，前期主要定价全球流动性收敛、逆全球化、 AI 产业链供
需关系等，对于国内政策和基本⾯的定价相对不⾜，⾦融街论坛期间推出的⼏项举措有利于稳
定增⻓条件，顺周期和内需相关资产将得到⼀定⽀撑，所以应告别单线索、适度提升配置⼴谱

性。
本⽂来源：郭磊宏观茶座

台積電 2330 TT  ( 電子–半導體 ) 
積厚成勢，晶耀巔峰 
投資與評價 
1. 台積電 3Q26 受惠 AI GPU、ASIC、CPU 及網通晶片需求強勁，HPC 營收
季增 22%，N2 營收占比提升至 8%，營運創新高；4Q26 隨 N2 於新竹及
高雄持續快速放量，N3 維持高稼動率，營運續創歷史新高。 
2. 台積電 26 年受惠 AI/HPC 需求強勁，N3 維持高稼動 、N2 加速放量，A16
於 2H26 量產，CoWoS 產能持續擴充，預估 EPS 110.83 元；預估 27 年
N2 年產能達 2.4 倍，台灣南科及美國亞利桑那 N3 新廠陸續量產，AI 加
速器與 Agentic AI 需求續增，EPS 151.11 元。 
3. 目前台積電股價交易在 2,440 元(16.1X 27(F) PER)，位於近三年歷史本益
比參考區間 12~26x 中間偏下位置，因 2 奈米量產爬坡以及海外廠折舊稀
釋，27 年毛利率將小幅衰退，本益比由中間偏上之 23 倍下調至 22 倍，
因調高 27 年獲利預估，故維持強力買進投資建議，目標價由3,220 元(23 
x 修正前 FY27E EPS)調升至 3,320 元(22 x 修正後 FY27E EPS)。 
重點更新 
台積電 3Q26 受惠 AI 及先進製程需求強勁，優於預期且創歷史新高 
N2 製程自 4Q25 量產後持續擴大產能，帶動 3Q26 HPC 與智慧型手機營收分
別季增 22%及 10%，占整體營收比重達 68%及 21%。在先進製程占比提升及
晶圓出貨量增加帶動下，營收 1.494 兆元(+17.6% QoQ) 。雖然 N3 產能利用
率維持高檔，加上整體營運規模持續擴大，惟 N2 初期量產仍面臨較高的製造
成 本 與 折 舊 負 擔 ， 形 成 稀 釋 ， 但 稀 釋 程 度 低 於 我 們 原 先 預 期 ， 毛 利 率
66.4%(-1.3ppts QoQ) ，營業利益 8,764.45 億元(+15.1% QoQ) ，稅後淨利
7,633.41 億元(+8.0% QoQ)，EPS 29.43 元，優於預期且創歷史新高。  
預期台積電 4Q26 受惠 AI 需求持續擴張，N2 放量推升營運再創新高 
隨 CSP 持續增加 AI 基礎建設投資，AI 加速器、Agentic AI 相關 CPU 及網通
晶片對先進製程的需求持續增加，上修 4Q26 營收預估 9.5%至 1.699 兆元
(+13.8% QoQ)。N2 處於快速擴產階段，初期量產成本及新增折舊仍對毛利率
形成壓力，加上海外晶圓廠持續稀釋毛利率，但先進製程需求強勁有助維持高
產能利用率，且 N3 製程量產規模擴大，小幅上調毛利率預估 0.1 個百分點至
66.3%(-0.1ppts QoQ)。調高營業利益至 9,911.86 億元(+13.1% QoQ)，上修
稅後淨利至 8,315.29 億元(+8.9% QoQ)，EPS 32.07 元，營運續創歷史新高。

### Page 2

報告內容 
台積電 3Q26 受惠 AI 及先進製程需求強勁，優於預期且創歷史新高 
受惠 AI 相關需求持續強勁 ，Agentic AI 逐步由傳統聊天機器人朝 AI 助理發展 ， 帶動AI 加速器、
CPU 及網通晶片需求同步增加，加上智慧型手機新產品備貨需求，以及 N2 製程自 4Q25 量產
後持續擴大產能，帶動 3Q26 HPC 與智慧型手機營收分別季增 22%及 10%，占整體營收比重
達 68%及 21%。以製程別觀察，N2 營收占晶圓營收比重由 2Q26 的 3%提升至 8%，N3 亦由
30%提升至 33%，N7 及以下先進製程合計占比達 79%，顯示先進製程需求持續成為主要成長
動能，3Q26 以 12 吋約當晶圓計算之出貨量季增 3.2%至 447.6 萬片。 
在先進製程占比提升及晶圓出貨量增加帶動下 ， 營收1.494 兆元(+17.6% QoQ)， 優於預期3.0%。
雖然 N3 產能利用率維持高檔，加上整體營運規模持續擴大，惟 N2 初期量產仍面臨較高的製
造成本與折舊負擔 ， 形成稀釋 ，但稀釋程度低於我們原先預期 ，毛利率 66.4%(-1.3ppts QoQ)。
隨 N2、A14 等先進製程持續開發，研發費用增加至 889.79 億元，推升營業費用至 1,159.34
億元(+17.1% QoQ)，營業利益 8,764.45 億元(+15.1% QoQ)，營業利益率 58.7%，稅後淨利
7,633.41 億元(+8.0% QoQ)，EPS 29.43 元，優於預期且創歷史新高。

預期台積電 4Q26 受惠 AI 需求持續擴張，N2 放量推升營運再創新高 
AI 相關需求延續強勁成長趨勢，隨 CSP 持續增加 AI 基礎建設投資，AI 加速器、Agentic AI 相
關CPU 及網通晶片對先進製程的需求持續增加 ， 加上N2製程於新竹及高雄廠區持續擴大產能，
預期將成為 4Q26 主要營收成長動能。雖然部分消費性電子及價格敏感型終端市場受到零組件
價格上漲與總體經濟不確定性影響，需求相對疲弱，但 HPC 與智慧型手機對 N2、N3 等先進
製程的需求仍維持強勁，且台積電持續透過 N5 設備轉換支援 N3，以及 N7、N5、N3 跨製程
節點產能優化，提高晶圓產出與產能利用效率。

### Page 3

上修 4Q26 營收預估 9.5%至 1.699 兆元(+13.8% QoQ) 。N2 處於快速擴產階段，初期量產成
本及新增折舊仍對毛利率形成壓力 ， 加上海外晶圓廠生產成本相對較高 ， 持續帶來毛利率稀釋，
但先進製程需求強勁有助維持高產能利用率，且 N3 製程量產規模擴大與生產效率改善有助降
低單位製造成本，小幅上調毛利率預估 0.1 個百分點至 66.3%(-0.1ppts QoQ) 。隨先進製程研
發投入增加、全球產能擴張及營運規模持續成長，調升營業費用預估 16.9%至 1,364.36 億元
(+17.7% QoQ)，調高營業利益至 9,911.86 億元(+13.1% QoQ)，營業利益率 58.3%，上修稅
後淨利預估 7.3%至 8,315.29 億元(+8.9% QoQ)，EPS 32.07 元，營運續創歷史新高。

先進製程與 CoWoS 持續供不應求，預期台積電 26 年營運創新高 
隨全球 CSP 持續擴大 AI 資料中心建設，AI 加速器、客製化 ASIC 及高速網通晶片需求持續增
加，加上 Agentic AI 逐步由聊天機器人朝具備自主執行能力的 AI 助理發展，進一步帶動 CPU
及先進製程運算晶片需求，推升台積電 HPC 業務持續成長。製程方面，N3 持續受惠 HPC、智
慧型手機及 AI 相關需求，產能利用率維持高檔，N2 自 4Q25 正式量產後，於 26 年持續擴大
新竹及高雄廠區產能，逐步提高先進製程營收貢獻。CoWoS 先進封裝需求持續強勁，台積電
已具備 5 倍光罩尺寸封裝量產能力，並朝 9 倍及 14 倍光罩尺寸推進，以滿足 AI 加速器晶片尺
寸與 HBM 整合需求持續提升的趨勢，預估 26 年年底 CoWoS 月產能達 14 萬片。 
在先進製程及先進封裝需求同步成長下 ，上修26 年營收預估3.5%至5.598 兆元(+47.0% YoY)。
受惠 N3 製程產能利用率提升、產品組合朝高階製程轉移，以及整體營運規模擴大帶來的製造
效率與成本改善，有助抵銷N2 初期量產及海外晶圓廠成本稀釋，其中海外廠預估對26 年全年
毛利率造成約 2 個百分點影響，維持資本支出 620 億美金之預估，上調毛利率預估 0.2 個百分
點至 66.7%(+6.8ppts YoY) 。調升營業利益預估 3.4%至 3.286 兆元(+69.8% YoY) ，調高稅後
淨利預估 3.8%至 2.874 兆元(+67.6% YoY)，EPS 110.83 元，營運創歷史新高。

### Page 4

預期台積電 27 年 N2 全面放量與全球產能陸續開出，營運再創新高 
隨全球 CSP 持續增加 AI 基礎建設投資 ，AI 運算需求由訓練逐步延伸至推論及 Agentic AI 應用，
帶動 AI 加速器、CPU 及網通晶片需求持續增加，使先進製程及先進封裝需求維持成長。製程
方面，我們預估 27 年 N2 全年產能較 26 年增加至 2.4 倍，主要由智慧型手機及 HPC/AI 客戶
需求推動，同時透過製造效率提升增加有效晶圓產出。N3 方面，受惠AI 及 HPC 客戶多年期需
求能見度提高，持續增加全球 N3 產能，南科新增 N3 晶圓廠預計 1H27 量產，且因客戶需求
持續增加，決定再新增一座 N3 廠，預計 1H28 量產；美國方面，亞利桑那第二廠已完成廠房
建設，目前進入設備搬入及安裝階段，預計 3Q27 開始量產，時程較原先規劃提前，日本 P2
則規劃於 28 年量產。隨 AI 加速器封裝尺寸持續增加，CoWoS 朝 9 倍、14 倍光罩尺寸發展，
CoPoS 亦持續開發，有望支援未來更大尺寸及更高整合度的 AI 晶片需求，27 年年底 CoWoS
月產能由原先預估之 19 萬片/月上修至 20 萬片/月。 
因先進製程持續供不應求，上修 27 年營收預估 12.3%至 8.143 兆元(+45.5% YoY)。為了因應
擴產需求，27 年資本支出由原先預估至 800 億美金上修至 850 億美金(+37.1% YoY) ，N2 持
續快速擴產、新增產能折舊增加，以及亞利桑那第二廠開始量產，海外晶圓廠成本稀釋壓力將
逐步提高，但上調 N3 及 N2 產能利用率，且先進製程占比提升有助維持產品組合優勢，調高
毛利率預估 0.5 個百分點至 66.5%(-0.1ppts YoY) ，調升營業利益預估 9.2%至 4.673 兆元
(+42.2% YoY)，營業利益率 57.4%，上修稅後淨利預估 7.8%至 3.918 兆元(+36.3% YoY)，EPS 
151.11 元，營運續創新高。

### Page 5

Agentic AI 擴大運算需求，台積電技術持續領先業界 
隨全球 CSP 持續擴大 AI 資料中心資本支出，GPU、AI ASIC、Agentic AI 相關 CPU 及網通晶
片需求同步成長 ， 台積電N3 產能利用率維持高檔 ，N2 進入快速放量階段 ， 雖然N2 初期量產、
海外晶圓廠擴張及新增折舊將對毛利率形成壓力，但先進製程營收占比提升、規模經濟效益、
生產效率改善及跨節點產能最佳化，有助於部分抵銷成本稀釋影響。亞利桑那州總投資規模 達
2,650 億美元，並持續評估德州擴產機會，反映公司對 AI 長期結構性需求的高度信心，亦有助
鞏固其在全球先進製程及高階晶圓代工市場的競爭優勢。 
目前台積電股價交易在 2,440 元(16.1X 2 7(F) PER) ，位於近三年歷史本益比參考區間 12~26x
中間偏下位置，考量預期台積電在 26~27 年間將持續實現強勁的營運增長 ，且 AI 由 Generative 
AI 邁向 Agentic AI，造成 Token數量暴增，運算需求大幅提升，但因 2 奈米量產爬坡以及海外
廠折舊稀釋毛利率，27 年毛利率將小幅衰退，故本益比由中間偏上之 23 倍下調至 22 倍，因
調高 27 年獲利預估，故維持強力買進投資建議，目標價由 3,220 元(23 x 修正前 FY27E EPS)
調升至 3,320 元(22 x 修正後 FY27E EPS)。

### Page 6

公司簡介 
台灣積體電路製造股份有限公司(TSMC)是全球領先的專業半導體代工廠，成立於 1987 年，總
部位於台灣新竹科學園區。TSMC 的核心業務是提供先進的晶圓製造服務，專注於為各大半導
體設計公司提供生產支援，而不從事自有品牌的產品設計或銷售。這使得 TSMC 能夠專注於技
術創新和製程優化，成為全球最重要的半導體代工廠之一。

自成立以來，TSMC 便致力於開創並發展純晶圓代工模式，這一模式徹底改變了半導體產業的
競爭格局，並且成為全球各大半導體設計公司合作的首選。公司在製程技術上的不斷創新，使
得其在全球市場中保持著領先地位 ， 尤其在先進製程領域如3 奈米和即將量產的 2 奈米製程上，
TSMC 更是無可匹敵的領頭羊。

圖 2. Revenue by Application

資料來源: TSMC、國泰證期研究部整理

TSMC 的客戶遍及全球，涵蓋了許多領先的半導體設計公司，包括蘋果、英偉達、超微、聯發
科等。這些企業依賴 TSMC 的製程技術來生產高效能運算、智能手機、AI 加速器以及車用電子
等各類產品。公司長期以來不斷擴大其生產能力，並在台灣及全球設立了多座先進的製造廠，
這些廠房均採用最先進的技術，並且能夠應對不斷增加的市場需求。

截至最近幾年，TSMC 的年產能已達 1600 至 1700 萬片 12 吋晶圓，並且在全球半導體市場的
市佔率持續穩居領先地位。這一市場地位使得 TSMC 不僅是全球晶圓代工市場的領導者，同時
也是半導體生態系統中不可或缺的核心企業。

### Page 7

台積電 ESG 政策 
台積電主要從事晶圓代工，產業類別歸類在半導體行業中，依據專門為投資人設計的永續揭露
框架 SASB 準則，半導體行業關注重點為下列幾項：溫室氣體排放、能源管理、水和廢水管理、
廢棄物和危險材料管理、員工健康與安全、員工多元化和包容性、產品設計與生命週期管理、
材料採購與效率。由於台積電有設立工廠從事生產，研究部將篇幅著重在溫室氣體排放、水和
廢水管理 ， 並因員工對於晶圓代工而言是相當重要的資產 ， 故員工相關議題也會著重於下描述。

1. 溫室氣體排放： 
民國 111 年台積公司溫室氣體總排放量約 1,800 萬公噸二氧化碳當量，來自含氟溫室氣體與
氧化亞氮等範疇一製程直接排放占 11%；電力使用造成的範疇二能源間接排放占 50%，仍是
最大排放源 ； 以原物料生產 、 能源上游相關活動與運輸為主的範疇三價值鏈間接排放則占 39%。
因先進製程產 
能持續擴充，溫室氣體整體排放較前一年上升 14%，但考量產能增長比例，單位產品排放量則
較前一年微幅下降 ， 顯示台積公司持續精進低碳製造作為 ， 仍有效降低單位產品溫室氣體排放。

圖 3. 台積電溫室氣體排放淨零路程 
資料來源:TSMC、國泰證期研究部整理

2. 水和廢水管理： 
民國 111 年整體回收系統增加 335 萬立方公尺節水量，晶圓單位產品用水量 137.3(公升／十
二吋晶圓當量─光罩數)，相較於基準年(民國 99 年)的 140.9(公升／十二吋晶圓當量─光罩數)
下降 2.6%，未達年度目標，主因為民國 111 年成立新廠區且為試產階段，雖然產能尚不具經
濟規模，但仍需一定程度的用水量，導致節水率下降；至於單位產品廢水排放量則為  93.0(公
升／十二吋晶圓當量─光罩數)， 相較前一年上升 18%， 同樣與新建廠區試產階段用水需求有關 ，
在新建廠區產能達到經濟規模前，台積公司將持續優化回收水系統設備操作參數、提升用水效
率，降低廢水排放量。

### Page 8

圖 4. 歷年節水量                            圖 5. 水資源回收量以及回收率

資料來源:TSMC、國泰證期研究部整理             資料來源:TSMC、國泰證期研究部整理 
3. 員工健康與安全： 
民國 111 年台積公司安全績效指標藍燈(優等)比例佔86%， 較民國110 年比例再提升 9.8%，
主要改善在於被動指標的虛驚事件數自前一年的 28 件下降至 17 件，發生千人率由民國 110 
年的 0.252 下降至 0.145。當發生虛驚事件時， 除使用 3L5W 品質分析工具進行調查，亦透
過虛驚事件通報及調查程序，訪談事件相關人員、分析及檢查設備、物料測試、相關文件與記
錄查核或重建現場以鑑別事件的直接原因、間接原因與基本原因，主動挖掘避免類似事件再發
的改善機會。

圖 6. 歷年事件數

資料來源:TSMC、國泰證期研究部整理

4. 員工多元化和包容性： 
為促進人才多元化、廣納特殊專業人才，台積公司持續在全球招募海外菁英，多年來與美國麻
省理工學院、美國史丹佛大學、美國加州大學柏克萊分校、日本東京大學及多所國際頂尖大學
密切合作，透過共同規畫研究專案培養尖端研究生、提早接觸優秀學子，以利未來延攬加入台
積公司。此外，半導體人才招募範圍亦拓展至美國各城市、新加坡與馬來西亞，並首次擴及歐
洲資訊人才，藉由線上說明會與實體招募活動，吸引人才加入。 
民國 111 年，全球招募 1 萬 2,442 位新進員工，新增 7,817 個優質工作機會，提供員工有競爭
力的整體薪酬、優於法令的福利制度、安全健康的工作環境，讓同仁樂於投入工作、學習與成
長，獲得歸屬感及成就感；並透過新人關懷措施，適切回應其需求，成功降低新進員工離職率。

聯友金屬 7610 TT (綠能環保) 
供應跟不上需求擴大 
投資與評價 
1. 3Q26 受惠國際鎢供應持續吃緊、鎢價維持高檔，加上屏東二廠新增
30%產能陸續開出，自售比重持續提升，預估營收季增 47.5%，毛利率
66.5%，EPS 27.32 元創單季新高。 
2. 2027 年成長動能由鎢價轉向擴產與產品高值化，屏南三廠、屏科廠陸
續開出，產品由鎢酸鈉延伸至 APT、氧化鎢、鎢粉及碳化鎢，自售比重
有機會挑戰 60%，預估 2026/27 EPS 83.77/109.27 元。 
3. 目前股價評價位於近兩年本益比 10～24 倍區間中段，考量非中鎢供應
鏈稀缺性、自售比重提升及 2027 年新產能接棒，給予區間中間偏上的
22 倍 FY27E PER，給予強力買進評等，目標價 2,400 元(22x FY27E 
EPS)。

重點更新 
3Q26 鎢價與自售業務推升獲利續增 
預估 3Q26 營收 28.43 億元（+47.5% QoQ） ，主要受惠國際鎢價維持高檔、
鎢酸鈉自售業務成長，以及屏東二廠新增 30%產能陸續開出；毛利 18.90 億
元（+44.8% QoQ） ，毛利率66.5%（-1.3ppts QoQ） ，營收與毛利仍維持高
速成長 。 營業費用1.88 億元 （+55.6% QoQ） ， 稅後淨利13.27 億元 （+40.3% 
QoQ），EPS 27.32 元創單季新高。預估 4Q26 隨新產能進一步量產、自售比
重過半，營收29.59 億元 （+4.1% QoQ） ，惟費用投入增加，EPS 降至 26.17
元。 
2027 年產能與高值化產品接棒 
預估 2027 年營收 132.79 億元 （+52.1% YoY） ，成長動能由鎢價逐步轉向產
能擴張及產品高值化，屏南三廠與屏科廠新產能陸續開出，產品由鎢酸鈉延
伸至 APT、氧化鎢、鎢粉及碳化鎢，自售比重有機會挑戰 60%；毛利 87.22
億元（+48.9% YoY） ，毛利率65.7%（-1.4ppts YoY） 。惟新廠與新工序投入
增加 ， 營業費用20.62 億元 （+202.8% YoY） ， 使營業利益66.60 億元 （+28.7% 
YoY） 、 營益率50.2%（-9.1ppts YoY） ； 稅後淨利53.09 億元 （+30.4% YoY），
EPS 109.27 元再創年度新高。後續營運重點將轉向新產能利用率，以及高值
化產品的提升速率。

### Page 2

訪談內容 
3Q26 鎢價與自售業務推升獲利續增 
預估 3Q26 營收 28.43 億元（+47.5% QoQ） ，主要受惠國際鎢供應持續吃緊、鎢價維持高檔，
加上鎢酸鈉自售業務成長 ， 以及屏東二廠增加的30%新產能部分陸續開出 ； 國際市場中國持續限
制鎢相關產品出口，尤其對美國與日本出口限制趨嚴，使國際客戶加速建立非中供應鏈，聯友以
二次硬質合金回收生產鎢產品，在競爭者有限的情況下，可望維持穩定的需求與產品售價。預估
毛利 18.90 億元（+44.8% QoQ） ，毛利率66.5%（-1.3ppts QoQ） ，雖自售產品比例提高，但
毛利率較 2Q26 高基期略降 ； 營業費用1.88 億元 （+55.6% QoQ） ， 營業利益17.02 億元 （+43.7% 
QoQ） ，營益率59.9%（-1.6ppts QoQ） 。預估稅後淨利13.27 億元 （+40.3% QoQ），EPS 27.32
元創歷史新高。除鎢產品外，公司亦持續推進循環再製鈷粉客戶認證與出貨，作為後續產品高值
化方向。

圖 1：聯友金屬獲利預估

資料來源: 國泰證期研究部整理

4Q26 營收續創高峰 
預估 4Q26 營收 29.59 億元（+4.1% QoQ） ，從上季大幅成長後仍維持季增，主要受惠鎢產品自
售業務持續成長，以及預期國際鎢供需仍偏緊，且公司新產能進一步進入量產階段，使單季自售
比重過半 。 中國掌握全球主要原生鎢供應 ， 出口限制使海外供應鏈短期難以快速補足缺口 ， 軍工 、
半導體及 AI 相關精密加工需求則持續支撐國際鎢價。預估毛利 19.21 億元（+1.7% QoQ） ，毛
利率 64.9%（-1.5ppts QoQ） ；惟營業費用增至3.00 億元（+59.6% QoQ） ，在於新的工序流程
投資持續投入，使營業利益 16.22 億元（-4.7% QoQ） ，營益率54.8%（-5.1ppts QoQ） 。預估
稅後淨利 12.71 億元（-4.2% QoQ），EPS 26.17 元；整體而言 4Q26 營收與毛利仍向上，但費
用增加使獲利較 3Q26 高峰略為回落。

### Page 3

2026 年鎢價大漲與自售比例增加 
預估 2026 年營收 87.31 億元 （+521.2% YoY） ，受惠國際鎢價大幅上漲、鎢酸鈉自售業務放量，
以及非中鎢供應鏈需求快速提升。中國鎢出口限制使國際市場供給趨緊，聯友具備廢硬質合金回
收、濕法冶金及既有海外客戶基礎，使鎢價上漲透過自售產品，直接轉化為獲利成長。預估毛利
58.57 億元 （+1,254.0% YoY） ， 毛利率由2025 年的 30.8%大幅提升至 67.1%（+36.3ppts YoY），
主因仍在產品國際報價及自售業務成長明顯；營業費用 6.81 億元（+452.3% YoY） ，營業利益
51.76 億元（+1,573.7% YoY） ，營益率59.3%（+37.3ppts YoY） 。預估稅後淨利40.70 億元
（+1,587.1% YoY），EPS 83.77 元創歷史新高，2026 年為公司材料處理量由加工大幅轉向自售
產品後，獲利結構明顯轉變。

圖 2：鎢金屬價格走勢

\

資料來源：Fastmarkets, 國泰證期研究部整理

2027 年產能與高值化產品接棒 
預估 2027 年營收 132.79 億元（+52.1% YoY） ，在2026 年鎢價與自售業務快速成長後，2027
年成長動能逐步由價格轉向產能擴張及產品高值化。公司規劃由既有鎢酸鈉進一步向APT、氧化
鎢、鎢粉及碳化鎢延伸，屏南三廠與屏科廠相關新產能預計陸續開出，鎢相關加工產能有機會擴
增倍翻，若產品認證與量產順利，可望提高公司在鎢產業鏈的垂直整合程度。預估毛利87.22 億
元（+48.9% YoY） ，毛利率65.7%（-1.4ppts YoY） ，在營收快速擴張下仍維持六成以上高毛利
率；惟配合新廠、新產品及營運規模擴張，營業費用增至 20.62 億元（+202.8% YoY） ，使營業
利益 66.60 億元（+28.7% YoY） ，營益率降至50.2%（-9.1ppts YoY） 。預估稅後淨利53.09 億
元（+30.4% YoY），EPS 109.27 元再創年度新高；2027 年營收與毛利仍維持高速成長，但費用
投入增加使獲利增速低於營收，後續營運重點將轉向新產能利用率，及APT、氧化鎢、鎢粉、碳
化鎢等高值化產品的提升速率。

### Page 4

非中鎢供應鏈稀缺性提升 
中國鎢出口限制使國際供應持續偏緊，聯友藉二次硬質合金回收與濕法冶金技術，受惠國際客戶
加速建立非中供應鏈，2026 年鎢價上漲、自售比重提升及屏東二廠新增產能開出，帶動獲利明
顯成長；2027 年成長動能則由格進一步轉向產能擴張與產品高值化，隨屏南三廠及屏科廠新產
能陸續開出，產品由鎢酸鈉延伸至 APT、氧化鎢、鎢粉及碳化鎢，自售部分有機會在 2027 年挑
戰 60%。預估 2026/27 EPS 為 83.77/109.27 元。3. 目前股價評價位於近兩年本益比 10～24
倍區間中段，考量非中鎢供應鏈稀缺性、自售比重提升及 2027 年新產能接棒，給予區間中間偏
上的 22 倍 FY27E PER，給予強力買進評等，目標價 2,400 元(22x FY27E EPS)。

產業概況 
鎢金屬需求持續由工業與國防驅動 
2026 年中國鎢總供給約 8.51 萬噸 W、YoY +12.4%，其中原生礦約 4.36 萬噸、幾乎零成長，回
收鎢則由高價刺激增加約 50%至約 3.0 萬噸 ， 使回收料占供給由2025 年的 25–26%升至約 35%；
換言之 2026 幾乎所有可快速增加的供給彈性都來自回收處理，使得非新礦山供應的競爭也明顯
升高。顯示高鎢價已開始抑制價格敏感度較高的傳統用途，但切削工具、航太與國防等剛性需求
仍維持成長。全球鎢需求主要仍來自硬質合金，應用於切削工具、礦山鑽頭、汽車與精密加工；
其中礦業與營建約占 2025 年全球 WC 市場 38.5%。 
國防需求的重要性在 2026 年進一步提高，美國與以色列對伊朗軍事行動、俄烏戰爭持續消耗飛
彈與穿甲彈用鎢，這類用途與硬質合金不同，使用後無法回收，使其對原生鎢形成永久性消耗。
因全球每年鎢需求約 10～11 萬噸，其中原生礦約占 65%、回收料約 35%，現有需求須依賴二
次資源補足。短期新礦與冶煉產能建置時間長，因此即使高價刺激新增供給，供需緊張仍不容易
快速逆轉。 
各應用來看硬質合金(Cemented carbide)佔比最大，主要用於切削刀具、採礦鑽頭、耐磨零件與
國防彈藥等。加工材/材加工品（Mill products） ：如鎢絲、鎢棒、鎢板等。鋼鐵與合金（Steel & 
alloys） 用於高速鋼、耐熱合金鋼等。化學品 （Chemicals） ：催化劑、顏料及化學試劑。其中 PCB
鑽針需求在近年也快速增加，據估計因 AI server PCB 變得更厚、更多層、更難鑽，高速低損耗
材料使 WC 鑽針磨耗大幅提高。AI server PCB 已朝 28–46 層甚至更高層數發展，而一般鑽針
原本約可加 工 3,000 孔 ， 在高階材料下可能降到約600 孔 ， 在相同鑽孔量可能需要接近4–5 倍
鑽針消耗。半導體需求在 4Q26 仍然偏強，其中第一類是半導體製程本身使用的高純度鎢/WF₆
等材料，另一類是晶圓、封裝、設備與零組件加工所使用的 WC 超硬工具。整體而言隨著 AI 
GPU/ASIC、HBM 與先進封裝擴產，使高純度 W 與精密 WC 工具需求維持穩定成長。  
圖 3：鎢金屬主要應用

資料來源：S&P Global, 國泰證期研究部整理

### Page 5

中國七成產能限制鎢出口影響大 
全球鎢供應高度集中，在2025 年全球鎢精礦開採產量約 8 萬多噸，中國占約75%，且中國同時
掌握從礦山、APT、鎢粉到碳化鎢的完整加工鏈，鎢的供應風險除了礦源集中，更包含中游精煉
能力集中 。2H25 開始中國開始收緊鎢礦開採配額並實施出口管制後 ， 非中國市場供給明顯轉緊 ，
價格也顯著提升。 
全球鎢市場主要依賴中國供應，因此中國價格大致主導全球定價；但出口限制與供應鏈安全政策
出現後，國際買家開始建立中國以外的礦源、冶煉及回收體系，使得國際報價持續走高，鎢回收
目前約占全球供給三成，雖然產地來源風險較低，但是既有供應鏈的產能瓶頸，使得回收流程取
得鎢的價格持續走高。雖陸續有些新礦場宣告將投入開發，但仍需兩年以上時間準備，舊有礦場
的重啟也面臨到回收率與經濟性的挑戰，且對於環境衝擊高未必適合歐美國家，目前最為環保與
的作法仍是從回收鎢開始加工。透過廢鎢回收陸續轉化為鎢酸鈉、APT、氧化鎢、鎢粉、碳化鎢，
將有利於賣方市場的精鍊供應商，邁向更高值化的末端產品發展。

圖 4：鎢礦蘊藏分布

資料來源：USGS, 國泰證期研究部整理

美國積極多元化鎢礦來源 
3Q26 美國政府承諾向 Elmet Group 投入 US$450mn，並搭配 DLA 最高 US$2bn 鎢戰略庫存
採購，政策目標已從單純取得礦源轉向礦山+APT/精煉+零組件的垂直整合。US$450mn 投資將
用於擴張美國鎢製造能力 、 取得長期原料 、 投資美國及盟國礦山與建立新的refining/conversion
能力；其中初期資金約 US$200mn。另一方面 DLA 與 Elmet 簽訂最高 US$2bn IDIQ 合約，最
低保證採購 US$150mn，品項直接涵蓋鎢礦、鎢精礦及鎢酸鈉；Elmet 待新增礦山、offtake 與
processing capacity 形成後才向戰略庫存交貨，以避免政府囤貨擠壓既有民間供應。 EQ 
Resources 規劃向美國供應 4,000 噸鎢精礦；但 Nevada APT processing facility全面恢復營運
預計要到 2H28，因此 4Q26 政策首先增加的是戰略採購與長約需求，而非立即增加APT 現貨供
給。這代表短期甚至可能強化非中國的原料競爭，但預計要數年後後才有機會逐步改善美國中游
加工缺口。

### Page 6

鈷金屬供給仍有短缺風險 
2025 年全球鈷礦產量約 31 萬噸，其中剛果民主共和國約23 萬噸、占七成比重，全球約 50%儲
量亦集中於剛果 ， 而中國又掌握全球七成以上鈷精煉能力 ， 因此上游礦源與中游精煉均高度集中 。
需求方面 2025 年全球鈷消費動力電池占比超過 65%，EV 仍是最大需求來源；雖電池高鎳化、
低鈷化以及 LFP 滲透率提升，會限制單位電池鈷用量成長，但 IT 電池的使用仍以氧化鈷為主要
材料。而長期更值得注意的是航空發動機、燃氣渦輪、國防設備所使用的高溫合金，以及硬質合
金與高性能磁鐵需求。高溫合金目前約占鈷需求 15～20%，工業工具、硬質合金與催化劑約一
成，使鈷逐漸從單一新能源金屬轉變成兼具新能源與戰略材料屬性的關鍵金屬；在供應集中及可
追溯要求提高下，循環再生鈷、高純度鈷粉與非中國精煉能力的戰略價值亦將持續提高。

公司簡介 
聯友金屬不依賴原生鎢礦，而是以廢硬質合金、鎢鋼及研磨屑等二次料作為鎢來源，經濕式冶金
回收後製成鎢酸鈉（Sodium Tungstate） ，同時回收鈷，形成WC/鎢鋼廢料、鎢酸鈉、APT、氧
化鎢的城市礦山供應鏈。公司目前鎢酸鈉年產能約 6,000 噸，2025 年鎢酸鈉出貨約 4,728 噸、
YoY +8%，其中代工約占 52%、自售約 48%，相較 2024 年代工 42%、自售 58%，可看出自
有設備利用率已處相對高檔 ； 公司規劃透過新廠將鎢酸鈉產能進一步提高至約 12,000 噸/年、較
現有產能增加 100%， 若以鎢酸鈉Na₂WO₄·2H₂O 含鎢重量約 55.7%換算 ， 滿產相當於一年可處
理約 6,700 噸 W contained。公司並規劃進一步向下游 APT 與氧化鎢延伸，使產品由目前以鎢
酸鈉為主，逐步建立回收料、鎢酸鈉、APT、WO₃的垂直整合能力。

圖 5：聯友金屬產業地位

### Page 7

ESG 政策 
公司在社會面評分上屬中上，然而需在經濟與環境面與揭露面強化:  
經濟面: 股東權益方面，公司優點為董事長親自出席股東常會；股東會結束後公開揭露逐項議案
之詳細表決資訊。公司可加強處為當年度未召開 2 次以上法人說明會。 
法規遵循方面，公司於題項評估期間未有不合理或受主管機關處分之情事。獨立董事方面，公司
優點為至少 2 名獨立董事之連續任期不超過 9 年。公司可加強處為獨立董事席次未達董事席次
二分之一以上。董事會永續治理方面，公司優點為有設置永續發展相關委員會；有制訂環境、社
會及治理之中長期發展策略；當年度永續報告書經董事會通過。公司可加強處為負責永續發展之
最終負責人非董事長或總經理。董事會績效與薪酬方面，公司優點為將董事績效評估與薪資報酬
連結。公司可加強處為企業永續績效尚未與高階主管及董事薪酬進行連結。 
董事會多元性方面，公司優點為揭露董事會多元化政策並揭露落實情形；董事會成員具備不同專
業背景 。 公司可加強處為任一性別董事席次未達1/3。 永續創新方面 ， 公司優點為取得綠色認證 。
公司可加強處為未揭露綠色相關部門設置情形與職責；未揭露綠色/永續採購之政策或金額。 
此外，產業創新方面，公司優點為對其專利之智慧財產權進行管理與保護，並揭露量化績效。 
法規遵循方面，公司近 3 年並未受公平交易委員會懲罰。

環境面: 環境系統與治理方面，公司優點為積極將綠色概念融入營運過程，打造綠色價值鏈；榮
獲 ISO 14001 環境管理認證；將環境管理作為與聯合國永續發展目標(SDGs)連結。空氣管理方
面，公司優點為制定空氣管理政策、揭露空氣汙染排放數據；公司可加強處為未揭露量化空氣汙
染減量目標。法規遵循方面，評估期間未違反空氣汙染相關法規或已針對違反情形提出具體改善
措施。氣候變遷管理方面，公司可加強處為未說明董事會或管理階層對氣候變遷議題之監督頻率
或事項；未揭露使用具公信力的 1.5 °C 以下及 3 °C 以上溫度情境評估氣候風險；未揭露與供
應鏈或合作夥伴共同推動因應氣候變遷相關策略；未揭露氣候變遷對公司所產生的機會項目。 
溫室氣體管理方面，公司優點為揭露至少三年的溫室氣體範疇一與二排放量，並揭露涵蓋範圍或
佔營收比率。公司可加強處為未揭露溫室氣體管理專責單位及相關管理政策；未揭露範疇三排放
量；未揭露量化溫室氣體減量目標；未揭露是否通過碳盤查認證。節能管理方面，公司優點為揭
露用電或節能數據。公司可加強處為未揭露能源管理專責單位及相關管理政策；未揭露節能量化
目標；未揭露能源風險對營運的影響評估結果。再生能源管理方面，公司可加強處為未揭露再生
能源管理專責單位及相關推動政策；未揭露再生能源使用量或發電量；未揭露量化再生能源發展
目標。廢水管理方面，公司優點為制定廢水管理政策、揭露廢污水排放量；公司可加強處為未揭
露量化廢汙水減量目標。 
揭路面: ESG 揭露方面，公司優點為依據 GRI 準則編寫永續報告書；於永續報告書清楚說明揭露
範疇；建置英文網站，並包含財務、業務及公司治理相關資訊；於永續報告書揭露重大性分析細
節。公司可加強處為永續報告書未依據 SASB 進行對應揭露；未上傳英文版永續報告書於公開資
訊觀測站；永續報告書尚未經過第三方機構確信或保證；未於官網揭露利害關係人問卷，供利害
關係人回饋；未說明公司營運對應SDGs 目標之具體作為；未揭露使用雙重重大性分析重大性議
題。

宏观点评 
 【粤开宏观】前三季度经济形势快评 
2026 年 10 月 19 日  投资要点

近期报告  
《 【粤开宏观】9 月出口再提速： 涨价支撑

何未充分释放？ ——中国文旅消费的特
征、问题与制度成因》2026-10-07 
《 【粤开宏观】300 亿美元对等降税安排：
美国对华关税的“变”与“不变”》2026-09-27 
《 【粤开宏观】对解决地方财政问题的思
考：兼谈地方附加税的意义》2026-09-21

一、如何看待前三季度经济总体形势？ 
可以从亮点成绩、存在问题及解决对策出发，并将之置于全球经济环境下分
析。 
第一，当前全球经济仍处于“低增长、高通胀”的总体态势，全球性的贸易
摩擦、老龄化、贫富分化抑制了出口、投资和消费活动，制约了全球经济的
低增长；同时 AI 带来的资本开支和财富效益是新的增量。 
第二，当然还存在一系列亟待问题的问题，包括需求不足尤其是消费不足、
企业面临的拖欠账款影响经济循环、房地产市场仍在持续调整以及由此带来
的地方财政收支紧平衡。 
第三，国庆前后一系列增量政策陆续出台，伴随前期六张网建设部署以及新
型政策性金融工具，预计能较好稳定经济增长，当然最核心还是要通过改革
和政策来激发微观主体积极性，包括收入分配、财税体制、市场准入和产权
保护改革等。 
二、如何看待前三季度投资增速放缓？ 
当前基建投资增速放缓是提高投资效益背景下的主动调整以及优质项目相对
稀缺的共同结果，这是新旧动能转换过程中必经阶段，经济转型意味着要从
传统基建投资驱动转向新质生产力驱动、从投资于物转向投资于人于人，这
就导致传统基建的数据相对下行；在看到投资增速下行的同时也要看到投资
结构的优化，投资结构的优化有利于提高财政资金使用效益以及降低债务风
险。 
三、政策建议：从宏观数据导向到微观感受导向，着眼于三大主体 
理念层面，要坚持“耐心政策”，推动宏观政策从注重 GDP、投资等宏观数据
的短期改善， 转向更加注重居民、 企业和地方政府三大微观主体的实际感受。 
操作层面，要围绕居民、企业和地方政府三大主体，从六个方面着力改善收
入预期、经营环境和发展能力。

风险提示：宏观政策超预期、外部冲击超预期
